Back to the finding

Minor C05. 'Same logical row behavior' for K invites a whole-line read-modify-write that races with the shared block save

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Every citation in the finding checks out against the primary sources at the named commits. Source checks (all via git show, no checkout): - h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-30 reads verbatim: "Token i owns column (i % 16) of token block (i / 16) in every panel. So tokens are written independently (no read-modify-write between tokens, unlike the pair-interleaved V plane)." - k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:135-146 scatter_row: comment "Only this token's 64 pair slots are written."; body is _mm512_i32scatter_epi32(detail::pair_base(plane, s, token), offsets, pairs, DWORD_BYTES_4) over 4 dimension steps. So a K row write touches 64 dword lanes in 64 separate 64-byte lines and never loads or stores a whole line. - h/tron/models/model.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2762-2765 Note [Shared block save] reads verbatim: "Items of one block that are not consecutive fall into several units. Their writers use masked stores on disjoint token lanes. So two threads may write one block without conflict." - Extra evidence the finding did not cite, which strengthens it: model.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2914-2927 stores a unit of one item with set_k_row (K_SCATTER_RUN_LEN_1), and kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:1704-1706 set_k_row calls k_vnni::scatter_row. So the ROW write path itself (not only store_block) runs concurrently with another thread's masked block store into the same 64 lines of the same 16-token block. A whole-line load/blend/store in that row path would overwrite the other thread's lanes with stale bytes (lost update) and is a C++ data race, while disjoint dword writes are distinct memory locations and are race-free. Design text checks (design-text.txt): line 262 "row.set(expr) | Write the logical row. Preserve the other row in its stored pair."; line 394 "An odd-token append reads the pair, changes one row, and writes the pair back."; line 488 "Add k_vnni_tensor, the blocked K storage owner, and k_vnni_view, its view. Give them the same logical row behavior." All quoted verbatim by the finding. One overstatement: the finding says "The design never states the write granularity rule for K rows." Design line 490, the paragraph right after the quoted sentence, says "Move K scatter and gather, which write and read logical rows, into the typed representation." That names the scatter as the K logical-row write, so the design does identify the mechanism. It does not state the constraint (no whole-line load/store in a K row write) or its reason (lane-disjoint concurrent writers), and the "must write only the token positions selected by its mask" rule at line 490 is given for the block write only. The ambiguity is real for the generic row.set(expr) path: the child converts non-bf16 sources to a stack bf16 row before scattering (model.hpp:2921-2924, 2935-2938), and an implementer following "same logical row behavior" plus the V row.set definition could implement that path as a per-chunk line read-modify-write. Severity stays minor: the adjacent "Move K scatter ... into the typed representation" sentence makes the wrong reading less likely, but the consequence of the wrong reading is a silent lost-update race in prefill, so the sentence should be made explicit as the finding recommends.
    Correction: The design does name the scatter as the K logical-row write ("Move K scatter and gather, which write and read logical rows, into the typed representation", design section 7). It does not state the write-granularity constraint (a K row write touches only its own token's 4-byte lanes and never loads or stores a whole 64-byte line) or the reason (the shared block save has several threads writing disjoint lanes of the same lines, and a run of one token goes through the row path concurrently, model.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2914-2927). "Give them the same logical row behavior", read together with the V row.set rule "Preserve the other row in its stored pair", leaves the generic row.set(expr) path open to a read-modify-write implementation.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's premises hold in the source. (1) h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-31 says verbatim "Token i owns column (i % 16) of token block (i / 16) in every panel. So tokens are written independently (no read-modify-write between tokens, unlike the pair-interleaved V plane)." scatter_row (same file, lines ~135-143) writes with _mm512_i32scatter_epi32 at DWORD_BYTES_4 scale, so only the token's 4-byte lanes. (2) h/tron/models/model.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6 Note [Shared block save] (lines 2732-2790) says "Items of one block that are not consecutive fall into several units. Their writers use masked stores on disjoint token lanes. So two threads may write one block without conflict." (3) The concurrency is real for single-row writes, not only for block writes: store_k_block (model.hpp:2850-2945) stores a run of one token with set_k_row (scatter, K_SCATTER_RUN_LEN_1 at line 2794) and longer runs with set_k_block (masked store). The child's own test (t/t_llama_unit.cpp@30c4ac82cbb6, SECTION "three threads: the helpers share the window and every row lands once") lists units [5,8) [8] [9,16) inside block 0, so a single-token scatter unit and masked block units of the same 64-byte lines run on different threads at the same time. A whole-line load/merge/store in place of the scatter would lose the other thread's lanes. (4) Main's V odd path is a whole-line read-modify-write: kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498:2306-2318 does _mm512_load_si512 of the pair line, _mm512_ternarylogic_epi32 merge, _mm512_store_si512. The design describes that path as the model for row.set ("Write the logical row. Preserve the other row in its stored pair", design section 5; "An odd-token append reads the pair, changes one row, and writes the pair back", section 6). Design-text fairness check: I read the whole design text. The design partly covers the finding at section 7 "Then update PR #4424": "Move K scatter and gather, which write and read logical rows, into the typed representation. Move the block write there too. It must write only the token positions selected by its mask." That sentence keeps the existing 4-byte-lane scatter as the K row write. But nothing in the design states the granularity rule (a K row write touches only its own token's lanes and never stores a whole line), nothing connects the K row write to the concurrent shared block save, and the "same logical row behavior" sentence points the implementer at V's row.set, whose specified behavior is a pair read-modify-write. The generic row.set(expr) path for k_vnni_row is therefore left open to a whole-line read-modify-write. The design's child verification row lists only "Writes to token positions excluded by the mask" as the detected failure, which a single-threaded test of a whole-line rewrite with identical neighbour bytes would pass. The design does not flag the write granularity as a deliberate choice, and the issue body's deferral ("keep ... shared-save coordination in #4424") covers counters and windows, not the write width of the typed K row. So the finding is not refuted; it needs the correction below to credit the "Move K scatter and gather" sentence.
    Correction: Ambiguity (child scope), narrowed: the design already tells the implementer to move the existing K scatter and gather into the typed representation, which preserves the 4-byte-lane write for the moved path. What is missing is (a) an explicit write-granularity rule for k_vnni_row so that the generic row.set(expr) path cannot be built as a whole-64-byte-line read-modify-write like V's odd path, (b) the reason: single-token scatter units and masked block units of the same 16-token block run on different threads at the same time (store_k_block run length 1 uses set_k_row inside a shared window; child t_llama_unit "three threads" section has units [5,8) [8] [9,16) in one block), and (c) a child verification entry for it. The recommendation's replacement sentence and test stand; the test can extend the child's existing three-thread shared-save section rather than add a new one.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Evidence checked, all holds: - The k_vnni.hpp quote is verbatim at h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-31 ("Token i owns column (i % 16) of token block (i / 16) in every panel. Issue #4525 keeps shared-save coordination in #4424; the finding's added sentence and test are child-scope, consistent with that. Where the finding overstates: the sentence "The design never states the write granularity rule for K rows" is not fully right. The very next design sentence names the mechanism: "Move K scatter and gather, which write and read logical rows, into the typed representation. Move the block write there too. It must write only the token positions selected by its mask." [design-text.txt:490]. That tells the implementer the row write is the 4-byte-lane scatter. What the design does not state is the invariant behind it (never load or store another token's lanes) or its reason (concurrent disjoint-lane writers). The gap matters most for the generic k_vnni_row.set(expr) path, where an implementer modelling on V's pair read-modify-write could load a line, blend, and store. Severity reasoning: child scope only; the parent PR is unaffected. An implementer who reads the code they are told to move sees the no-RMW property in the header comment [k_vnni.hpp:28-31].     Correction: Ambiguity (child scope), narrowed: the design does name the K row-write mechanism ("Move K scatter and gather, which write and read logical rows, into the typed representation. Move the block write there too. It must write only the token positions selected by its mask." [design-text.txt:490]), so it does not leave the granularity wholly unstated. What it omits is the invariant and its reason: a K logical-row write, including the generic k_vnni_row.set(expr) path, must touch only its own token's 4-byte lanes and never load or store a whole 64-byte line, because the shared block save (Note [Shared block save], model.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2764-2768) has several threads writing disjoint lanes of the same lines at the same time. "Give them the same logical row behavior" [design-text.txt:488] should be read as "same logical interface", not "same read-modify-write rule as packed V"; one sentence saying so closes the gap. The recommended two-thread test already exists in substance as the child's three-thread shared-save case [t/t_llama_unit.cpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2769-2810] and is covered by the design's "Child tests" row [design-text.txt:528]; a new row is optional, and a deterministic check (assert no full-line store in the row path, or compare the plane bytes outside the written lanes) would be stronger than another timing-dependent multi-thread case.

Minor F03. 'Mostly right' unexplained; overstates Ben's position

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Core claim verified against primary sources. Minor F13. Interface lacks packed-to-packed token copy for append_v

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: refuted (high confidence, severity minor). The evidence the finding cites is accurate, but its mechanism is wrong. Verified facts: append_v (the per-token packed-V copy between two cache pages) at f46e48bab498 h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1873-1920 has four branches; the two even-destination branches store zero into the odd partner (zero_b, zero_a), and the two odd-destination branches PRESERVE the even partner (store [b d], store [a d] keep d). copy_storage_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1927-1957) calls append_v once per token under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE==16 (line 1945). Its only caller is book::append via copy_from (kv_cache.hpp:1046, 1961), which the code comment calls defragmentation. append_v is the only packed-to-packed write at main (grep of .v[ accesses: set_v 2296/2335/2376 take source rows, get_v 2414/2448 read, scaled_v 2261 reads). The child 30c4ac82cbb6 kv_cache.hpp:2093-2144 does whole-page memcpy(dst_block.k, src_block.k, ...) else k_vnni::gather_row + k_vnni::scatter_row per token. All of that holds. What does not hold: (1) The finding says row.set(expr) "has the opposite partner rule (preserve instead of zero)". That is only true for even destinations. For odd destinations append_v also preserves the even partner, so row.set(expr) ("Preserve the other row in its stored pair", design-text line 262) is the exact match for two of the four branches. (2) For even destinations the design DOES name an operation with the zero-partner rule: "destination_view.set_pair(pair_index, even_row_expr, odd_row_expr)" and "Provide a form that adds an even token and zeros the odd row in the same pass. It must preserve the current store operation without reading destination bytes." (design-text lines 324, 330). The finding does not mention set_pair. (3) Both operations take row expressions, and the design makes a packed row a row expression: "The row inherits the vector expression interface expr<row_type, D>" (line 244), with "matrix[token].bf16_chunk(i) | Extract the stored bf16 bits directly. Avoid a float conversion during exact copies." (line 260) and "Generic packed expression assignment | ... Prefer bf16_chunk() to preserve the bits of bf16 sources." (line 410). So dst[odd].set(src[j]) and dst.set_pair(k, src[j]) (even-zero form) compose all four append_v branches bit-exactly without inventing a signature. (4) The failure scenario (implementer uses row.set on fresh even destinations and breaks the zero-partner invariant) is guarded by the design's own sentences right after the quoted one: "Keep clearing the odd row when writing an even destination token. Ordinary row assignment must instead preserve the adjacent row." (line 440). (5) The recommended copy_plane byte copy is already covered: "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout" (line 438) and "Owner copy / move | Copy the array stored inside the object ... Preserve trivial copyability" (line 270); at main the CHUNK-16 V path never does a whole-plane copy anyway (kv_cache.hpp:1942-1946). Residual: the design never states that copy_storage_slot's per-token loop maps onto set_pair (even destination) and row.set (odd destination), and step 7 says "Use typed append, copy, and logical row extraction operations" (line 466) while section 5 names no operation called "copy". An implementer could therefore either compose the two named operations or add a dedicated copy_token; both satisfy the stated behavior. That is an ambiguity, not a major omission.
    Correction: Ambiguity (minor): section 6 tells the implementer to move append_v's four branches "into the typed operation implementation" and step 7 speaks of a "typed ... copy" operation, but the design never says which named operation(s) copy_storage_slot's per-token loop should call. The design already names the two operations that together reproduce all four append_v branches: set_pair's even-token-with-zero-partner form for even destinations (zero the partner, no destination read) and row.set(expr) for odd destinations (preserve the even partner), both accepting a packed row as a row expression via bf16_chunk() for a bit-exact copy. Add one sentence to section 6 or step 7: "copy_storage_slot's per-token V copy calls set_pair (even-zero form) for an even destination token and row.set for an odd destination token, with the source row taken from the source page's const view; a whole-plane copy uses the owner's trivial copy." No new signature is required; the child adds the same mapping for K (gather_row/scatter_row become that type's row read/write, whole-page copy stays a byte copy).
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Source evidence holds. At main f46e48bab498, kv_cache.hpp:1871-1920 defines page::append_v with four even/odd branches; the two even-destination branches store zero_b / zero_a (partner zeroed). kv_cache.hpp:1927-1946 copy_storage_slot under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE==16 calls append_v<geometry> once per token. The only caller of copy_from is book::append(const book&) at kv_cache.hpp:1020-1046, which is the defragmentation path (the comment at 1020 says "Used for defragmenting books created by tree-shaped speculation"), so "every append and defragmentation" is one path, a wording detail. The other raw .v[] uses at main (lines 1681-1716 CHUNK-8 v()/v_ptr, 1952-1954 CHUNK-8 memcpy, 2261 scaled_v, 2296-2448 set_v/get_v) are not packed-to-packed copies, so append_v is indeed the only one. The child at 30c4ac82cbb6 kv_cache.hpp:2093-2116 copies K as one memcpy for a whole page, else k_vnni::gather_row + k_vnni::scatter_row per token, so the child will need the same operation shape for K. Design-text fairness check. Section 5 (design-text.txt:310-321) names append_v_row(destination_view, token, source_row_view) whose source is "a source view containing one token's consecutive dimension values" with Source in bf16, fp16, float. A packed-V source row is not consecutive, so no named signature covers the append_v case; that part of the finding stands. set_pair(pair_index, even_row_expr, odd_row_expr) (lines 324-330) could in principle express the even-destination cases via vnni_row.bf16_chunk(), but that changes today's in-place mask-and-store sequence to extract-then-repack, and the design does not say to use it for page copies. However, section 6 (line 440) already says "Move packed V append logic into the typed operation implementation. Preserve all four combinations of even and odd source and destination tokens. Keep clearing the odd row when writing an even destination token. Ordinary row assignment must instead preserve the adjacent row." That directly rules out using row.set for the copy, so the finding's failure scenario is guarded by the design, which lowers severity from major to minor. Step 7 (line 466) also says "Use typed append, copy, and logical row extraction operations", and line 346 says "Keep existing page methods where needed. Make them call the new logical operations", so page::append_v may remain as a thin page method. The recommended copy_plane is redundant with "Owner copy / move ... Preserve trivial copyability" (line 270) and "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout" (line 438). Net: the omission is real but narrow (a missing signature name), and the design's prose already prevents the wrong-implementation outcome the finding predicts.
    Correction: Ambiguity, not a major omission: section 5 of the design names no signature for the packed-to-packed per-token V copy that page::append_v (kv_cache.hpp:1871-1920 at f46e48bab498) performs today, so the implementing agent must choose a name and parameter list. The design does, however, already state where that logic goes and what it must preserve: "Move packed V append logic into the typed operation implementation. Preserve all four combinations of even and odd source and destination tokens. ... Keep clearing the odd row when writing an even destination token. Ordinary row assignment must instead preserve the adjacent row." (section 6). That sentence forbids the substitution the finding warns about (using row.set(expr) for the append copy), so the "would break the zero-partner invariant" scenario is already guarded in prose. The recommended copy_plane(owner&, owner const&) is redundant: the "Owner copy / move" row (trivially copyable owner, bytewise copy) and "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout" already cover whole-plane copies. What should be added is one line in the section 5 interface list naming a packed-source overload, e.g. append_v_row(vnni_view<bf16,R,D> dst, size_t dst_token, vnni_view<const bf16,R,D> src, size_t src_token), with the rule "even destination zeroes its partner, odd destination merges", implemented with today's four branches, and a note that PR #4424 adds the same operation for blocked K (child copy_storage_slot: whole-page memcpy else k_vnni::gather_row + k_vnni::scatter_row, kv_cache.hpp:2093-2116 at 30c4ac82cbb6).
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Code citations hold. append_v at f46e48bab498 h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1873-1923 has four even/odd branches, and both even-destination branches store zero_b / zero_a (partner zeroed). copy_storage_slot at 1944-1946 calls append_v once per token. Lines 1880/1882 are the only place a packed .v[...] is read from a source page and written to a destination page (grep over h/ and src/ at f46e48ba), so "the only packed-to-packed write" is correct. The child (30c4ac82cbb6 kv_cache.hpp:2099-2116) does need the same shape for K: whole-page memcpy else k_vnni::gather_row + k_vnni::scatter_row per token through a scratch row. Design omission is real. Section 5's operation table (matrix[][], chunk, bf16_chunk, row.set, matrix.set, view=, as_view, owner copy/move, owner expr ctor, const conversion) has no entry with a packed source token. The named append family is "append_v_row(destination_view, token, source_row_view)" whose source is "a source view containing one token's consecutive dimension values" with "source types are bf16, fp16, and float"; a vnni_row "must not represent those dimensions as consecutive values in memory", so it cannot be that source. Section 6 ("Move packed V append logic into the typed operation implementation. Preserve all four combinations...") and step 7 ("Use typed append, copy, and logical row extraction operations") refer to an operation the interface list never names. The implementer must pick a name and signature. Severity is overstated. The failure the finding fears (using row.set, which preserves the partner, on an even fresh destination) is forbidden twice in section 6: "Keep clearing the odd row when writing an even destination token. Ordinary row assignment must instead preserve the adjacent row." and "Ordinary row assignment reads and updates a pair, so that pair must already be initialized." The retained test catches it: t_llama_unit.cpp:2340-2427 at f46e48ba (TEST_CASE "scaled_v padding NaN", SECTION "append_v preserves poison") poisons token 7 with NaN, appends token 6 via append_v(dst=6, src=3), then REQUIRE_FALSE(isnan) on scaled_v with count 7 (reads pair 6,7). A preserve-partner copy leaves the NaN and fails the test. The design's verification table retains this test and asks for "all even/odd append combinations". So the implementer cannot build the wrong thing unnoticed; the gap only forces them to name one internal operation. Intent check. Ben's straw-man (#3) also names no packed-to-packed copy; it has store_row/load_row per layout, under which a per-token copy is load_row into scratch + store_row, exactly the child's K shape. A named copy_token is compatible with Ben's "usual operations that we expect of tensor types" and with jhan's "VNNI-K change ... a local change within the new tensor data type"; the finding does not push away from intent. But the design's silence leaves two acceptable shapes (direct four-branch op, or load+store / set_pair+row.set composition), so it is an ambiguity, not a wrong-build risk. Two parts of the recommendation are redundant or loose. copy_plane(owner&, owner const&) is already covered by the table row "Owner copy / move | Copy the array stored inside the object ... Preserve trivial copyability" plus section 6 "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout"; and at main V is never copied whole-plane (only the child's K is). "on every append and defragmentation" names one path twice: copy_from's only caller is book::append (kv_cache.hpp:1021-1046 at f46e48ba), whose comment says it is used for defragmenting tree-shaped speculation books.
    Correction: Omission (minor): section 5 names no operation for copying one token between two packed V views, although sections 6 and 7 tell the implementer to move append_v's four branches into the typed implementation. Add one interface row, for example copy_token(vnni_view<bf16,R,D> dst, size_t dst_token, vnni_view<const bf16,R,D> src, size_t src_token) with the rule "even destination zeroes its partner, odd destination merges", and note that copy_storage_slot reaches both views through kv_block_at_storage<geometry>(offset, kv_head).v.as_view() and that the child adds the same pair for K. The zero-partner risk the finding names is already blocked by section 6's text and by the retained "append_v preserves poison" test, so this is not a major defect. Drop the copy_plane suggestion: owner copy assignment (trivially copyable) already gives the whole-plane byte copy, and at main V never takes that path.

The design's "keep #4424 draft until its parent merges" implies the stacked form, but the design never states the base-branch step. Severity downgraded to minor. The step is the last one in the design, after the parent PR is complete. Either resolution produces the same child code and leaves the parent PR mergeable. The worst outcome is a noisy #4424 diff that one gh pr edit --base command fixes without code changes. The fix to the design is one sentence.

Minor F17. Generated-instruction comparison lacks baseline, tool and threshold

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding is partly right and partly overstated. I checked each of its five "missing" items against the design text and the tron source. The design lists three parent build configurations in the "Parent build configurations" row [design-text.txt line 526], but the performance paragraph does not say which one to disassemble. - No numeric threshold for "materially" exists anywhere in the page (grep for "material" hits only lines 332, 381, 386-387 of the HTML, none defines it). What does not hold: - "names no baseline commit": the design does name the baseline. It says "compare the parent with its base using the same main revision" [design-text.txt line 554]. It cannot name a SHA, because Section 7 tells the implementer to "Start a separate branch from an updated main" [line 450] and Section 3 says "Fetch and review newer main changes before implementation" [line 132]. The recommendation's "baseline = the parent's merge-base on main" restates what the design already says. - "no definition of a material change": the same paragraph gives qualitative criteria: "still use vector instructions. They must not allocate memory or clear the arena" [line 552]. Section 6 adds "Keep the current unrolled loops" and forbids "A loop that reads one scalar value at a time" [line 412]. Step 2 adds "Do not replace these vector operations with scalar loops" [line 456]. A qualitative definition exists; only a numeric one is missing, and the recommendation's "more than 10 percent" number has no source. - "no symbols": the design names the three operations (even-row write with partner clear, odd-row write, weighted V sum) [line 552] and names the functions set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v in Section 7 [lines 472-476]. Those map one-to-one onto kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498 set_v (lines 2289-2398), append_v (1873-1920) and scaled_v_expr (1990-2110), which I verified. Operation-level naming is the only workable level here, because the parent replaces set_v with new functions (append_v_row, set_pair), so old symbol names cannot be compared with new ones. The recommended procedure is also technically flawed. TRON(inline) expands to inline __attribute__((always_inline)) [h/common/attributes.hpp:74-78 @f46e48bab498]. set_v and append_v carry TRON(inline) [kv_cache.hpp:2287-2289, 1872 @f46e48bab498], so they never appear as standalone symbols in a linked t_llama_unit; their bodies land inside callers such as save_v_impl [model.hpp:2818-2820 @f46e48bab498]. "extract the symbols for page<...>::set_v" via objdump cannot work as written. Also -DAVX512=ON is redundant: option AVX512 defaults ON [CMakeLists.txt:44 @98bb8cb22f94] and the native preset probes the host [CMakePresets.json "native"]. Severity: neither jhan's prompt (codex-add-tensor-type.md) nor Ben's comments ask for a performance gate, and the design states the parent "makes no speedup claim" [line 552]. The residual gap (tool, build configuration, note that comparison must be on inlined caller bodies) is an ambiguity an implementing agent can resolve without building the wrong thing. That is minor, not major.
    Correction: Ambiguity (minor): the "Performance acceptance" instruction-comparison step names the baseline ("its base using the same main revision") and gives qualitative pass criteria (vector instructions kept, no allocation, no arena clearing, unrolled loops kept, no scalar loops), but it names no disassembly tool, does not say which of the three listed build configurations to compare, and does not warn that set_v/append_v are always_inline (TRON(inline)) so the comparison must be made on the inlined bodies inside callers such as save_v_impl and the append/copy path, not on standalone symbols. A numeric threshold for "materially" is absent, but no source supports any particular number; the qualitative criteria already in the design are the usable definition.

Three of the five claimed gaps are already covered by the design, so the finding overstates. They must not allocate memory or clear the arena" [552]; "Do not replace these vector operations with scalar loops" [456]; weighted V sum must keep "the existing order of additions" and "the current unrolled loops", and must not become "A loop that reads one scalar value at a time" [412]; "Check the generated code to confirm that construction does not initialize either plane" [428]. The finding's "more than 10 percent more instructions" threshold is arbitrary and is not evidence that these qualitative criteria are wrong. (3) Symbols: the design names the three operations to compare [552] and names the existing functions set_v, append_v, scaled_v_expr in steps 2/4 and the caller list [456-476]. Also, page::set_v and scaled_v_expr are TRON(inline) class-template members [kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498:2287-2290, 1988-1990], so in an optimized build they are normally inlined into save_v_impl and the attention callers; the finding's per-symbol objdump count would not reliably find them. Minor F30. State plainly that native K gets no new type

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). I re-derived every cited fact from the primary sources at f46e48bab498 (the main snapshot the design reviewed). All hold. Design text: the Short version says "Introduce this interface for the existing key and value caches before changing the key layout in pull request #4424" [design-text.txt:17]. Section 5 says "// Existing types remain the native-K representation." and defines native_k_view as an alias of the existing view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>> [design-text.txt:229-236]. Section 5 also says "Native K returns its existing row-major view." and "Keep native K on those existing types." [design-text.txt:342, 278]. Section 6 says "Native K may keep its existing array and view." [design-text.txt:420]. So the body and the Short version do differ in what they promise for K. The finding's ambiguity claim is real, and the finding correctly acknowledges that section 5 resolves it. Source facts, all at f46e48bab498: - kv_block::k_view() returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> (non-const) or const_view<...> (const); k_at(i) returns k_view()[i] [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2207-2231]. Native K already has a typed whole-page view. - page::k<geometry>(slot, kv_head) returns kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k_view(), in const and non-const forms [kv_cache.hpp:1556-1570]. page::k<geometry>(slot, kv_head, i_page) returns k<geometry>(slot, kv_head)[i_page] [kv_cache.hpp:1583-1599]. So a page-level whole-page K view already exists; the design's proposed page::k_tensor<geometry>(slot, head) duplicates it for native K. - save_k_impl writes K with pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(kout); where kout is a slice expression [h/tron/models/model.hpp:2754-2757]. Typed row write confirmed. - The software QK path does keys = page.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head); const auto k = keys[i]; s_pages[offset][i] = dot_q[offset](k); [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1545, 1580-1583]. dotter accepts view/const_view<U, aligned, dma, seq<size>, sseq<1>> [h/tron/kernels/dotter.hpp:16-34, 45-58]. Typed row view into dotter confirmed. - The AMX QK call site does const auto k0 = pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, 0); amx_attn_h128g4::qk_rowmajor_128x4(k0.data, q_packed, &s_pages[0][0], page::page_size); [self_attention.hpp:1423-1425]. This is the one place K leaves the typed world. - void qk_rowmajor_128x4(const bf16* k_page, ...) [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:219]; definition at src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:122; fake at t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:180-181 takes const bf16*; the numerics test passes a raw array in->k [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:158-159]. - The gof K callback returns .data as bf16* [h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:2648-2654]. The design keeps that interface in the parent [design-text.txt:446], which is consistent with the finding's "only K change" statement. C++ semantics check: pg is const page& in apply_dense_amx_page [self_attention.hpp:1396], so pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head) yields const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<64, 128>>, which is exactly the design's const_native_k_view<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128>. The finding's proposed change compiles in principle without a new type. Two loose points in the finding, neither of which refutes it: (1) the quoted phrase "the existing key and value caches" appears only in the Short version; the HTML <title> and <h1> say "Typed cache tensors" without naming K [design-new-tensor-type.html:7, 28], and the live issue title "Introduce typed KV-cache tensors for packed V and native K" is jhan's own rename (facts.md, 2026-09-22T00:10:29Z), though the issue body item "Add the abstraction for existing V and native K on main" is codex's text. (2) "The only K change" omits the mechanical follow-ons that the parameter change forces: the call site [self_attention.hpp:1423-1425], the kernel definition [amx_attn.cpp:122], and the numerics test call [t_amx_numerics.cpp:158]. The design already lists the fake and the numerics setup [design-text.txt:369]. Severity stays minor: the design body is unambiguous in three places, so the risk that an implementer builds a K wrapper is low, but the Short version does overstate the K scope and the redundant page::k_tensor accessor points the same way.
    Correction: The finding is right in substance. Tighten it in two places. Minor F34. Owner-is-an-expr unstated; rvalue-slice argument depends on it

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). I re-derived the finding from the design text and tron source at f46e48bab498. Every cited fact holds. Design text (design-text.txt line 244, verbatim): "The view inherits the matrix expression interface expr<view_type, R, D> directly. The row inherits the vector expression interface expr<row_type, D>. The owner implements the matrix expression operations by calling its view." The HTML contains only these two "inherits" statements plus the negative "Do not inherit from views<>" (design-new-tensor-type.html lines 176, 182). No sentence names the owner's base class. Line 268 requires "owner indexing" and line 272 an "Owner expression constructor / assignment", so the owner has operator[] and accepts expr inputs, but whether it IS an expr is unstated. The ambiguity is real. Precedent split, confirmed: h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:31 struct tensor<N> : expr<tensor<N>, N> {; tensor.hpp:236 struct tensor<d0, d1, ds...> { (no base); h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:990 struct alignas(chunk_alignment) aligned_vector : expr<aligned_vector<N>, N> {. Consequence 1 (direct construction), confirmed: tensor.hpp:272 tensor(expr<B, d0, d1, ds...> const& rhs), :278 operator=(expr<B, d0, d1, ds...> const& rhs), h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:70 dmatensor(TRON(noescape) expr<B, R, C, Ds...> const& other). Template argument deduction does not consider user-defined conversions, so tensor<64,128> t(owner) compiles only if vnni_tensor derives from expr<B,64,128>; otherwise the caller must write owner.as_view(). Consequence 2 (rvalue binding to the general slice), confirmed: h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:147-150 template <typename B, size_t in_d0, size_t in_d1, typename... Offsets> requires(d0 != DYN_SIZE && sizeof...(ds) == 1) auto operator()(expr<B, in_d0, in_d1> const& v_in, ...); :168-169 "// It's critical that v_in be captured by reference here." / auto f = [=, &v_in](size_t i) {. An rvalue owner deriving from expr<vnni_tensor,R,D> binds to this const& parameter by derived-to-base conversion and the returned lambda_expr holds a dangling reference; deleting as_view() && does not stop this. A non-expr owner fails deduction for this overload, so the design's stated justification ("The existing general expression slice accepts a const reference", line 290) applies only when the owner is an expr. The finding's reading of the C++ semantics is correct. slice.hpp:38-40 template <typename Owner, typename... Offset> requires requires(Owner& owner) { owner.as_view(); } auto operator()(Owner& owner, ...): a non-const rvalue deduces Owner = vnni_tensor and vnni_tensor& cannot bind it, as the finding says. One over-strong parenthetical, see correction. It does not change the verdict: the ambiguity exists, the design's justification sentence presupposes an expr owner, and the recommendation (state the base explicitly and list the deleted overloads as requirements) is the right fix.
    Correction: The parenthetical "otherwise the Owner& slice overload cannot bind an rvalue and the hazard does not exist" is too strong. For a const-qualified prvalue (a function returning const vnni_tensor<R,D>), deduction against Owner& at slice.hpp:40 yields Owner = const vnni_tensor, the parameter becomes const vnni_tensor&, which binds the materialized temporary; inside the body owner is an lvalue, so as_view() const& runs and the deleted as_view() && is bypassed. So "Deleting only as_view() && is insufficient" stays true for a non-expr owner via that edge case, but for a reason the design does not state. Corrected claim: the design never says whether vnni_tensor inherits expr<vnni_tensor, R, D>; the design's justification for "Deleting only as_view() && is insufficient" (the general expression slice's const& parameter) applies only if the owner is an expr, and direct construction tensor<R,D>(owner) / dtensor(owner) compiles only in that case. The recommendation stands unchanged. Also a nuance: the design's verification row "Construct an existing host tensor from the packed matrix expression" can be read as constructing from the view, so direct construction from the owner is a consequence the implementer faces rather than something the design explicitly relies on.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The design never states the owner's base class. The whole page has exactly two "expr<...>" mentions, both in one sentence: "The view inherits the matrix expression interface expr<view_type, R, D> directly. The row inherits the vector expression interface expr<row_type, D>. The owner implements the matrix expression operations by calling its view." [design-text.txt:244; design-new-tensor-type.html:176]. "Implements the matrix expression operations" fits two readings: (a) vnni_tensor inherits expr<vnni_tensor, R, D> and forwards operator[] to as_view(), or (b) vnni_tensor only provides operator[] like tron's rank-2 tensor, which has no expr base. The precedent is split, as the finding says, and I confirmed it at f46e48bab498: tensor.hpp:31 struct tensor<N> : expr<tensor<N>, N>, tensor.hpp:236 struct tensor<d0, d1, ds...> { with no base, expr.hpp:990 aligned_vector : expr<aligned_vector<N>, N>, and additionally dmatensor.hpp:35 rank-2 dmatensor inherits expr. Ben's comment #2 says "Follow tron precedent tensor/dtensor built on expr", so an implementer following the rank-2 tensor precedent would land on reading (b). The finding's second claim also holds: the design's "Deleting only as_view() && is insufficient. The existing general expression slice accepts a const reference" [design-text.txt:290] is only true under reading (a). slice.hpp:38-42 at f46e48bab498 is template <typename Owner, ...> requires requires(Owner& owner) { owner.as_view(); } auto operator()(Owner& owner, ...), and a non-const rvalue cannot bind to Owner&. slice.hpp:147-150 is operator()(expr<B, in_d0, in_d1> const& v_in, ...) and :168-169 says "It's critical that v_in be captured by reference here." Only an owner derived from expr reaches that overload and dangles. Nothing elsewhere in the design resolves this: line 78 ("The new type must also support the existing expression interface"), line 250 ("The matrix uses the matrix form of the expr interface") and the verification row at line 514 ("Construct an existing host tensor from the packed matrix expression") all name the view or an unspecified "type", not the owner. The design does not flag the owner's base class as a deliberate choice or a deferral, so the fairness lens does not refute it. Severity stays minor because the design's requirement at line 290 ("Reject slicing an rvalue owner with an overload marked as deleted or a type restriction") is unconditional, so an implementer is protected under either reading; the ambiguity only affects whether tensor<R,D>(owner) and dmatensor(owner) compile directly and whether the "insufficient" rationale makes sense.
    Correction: One nuance in the finding's parenthetical is overstated: "otherwise the Owner& slice overload cannot bind an rvalue and the hazard does not exist". A const rvalue owner (for example a function returning const vnni_tensor<R,D> by value) deduces Owner = const vnni_tensor<R,D>, and const vnni_tensor<R,D>& binds that temporary. Inside the overload, owner.as_view() then calls the const& overload and returns a read-only view into the expiring temporary. So without expr inheritance the hazard shrinks to const rvalue owners, but it does not vanish. The recommendation stands either way: state whether vnni_tensor inherits expr<vnni_tensor<R,D>, R, D>, and keep the deleted slice<...>::operator()(vnni_tensor<R,D>&&, ...) overload (or a type restriction) as a stated requirement in both readings.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding holds. Checked evidence: 1. The design never states the owner's base class. Design text: "The view inherits the matrix expression interface expr<view_type, R, D> directly. The row inherits the vector expression interface expr<row_type, D>. The owner implements the matrix expression operations by calling its view." Only the view and the row get an explicit base. "Implements ... by calling its view" can be read either as "inherits expr and forwards operator[]" or as "offers operator[] without an expr base". 2. The precedent split is real at f46e48bab498: h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:31 struct tensor<N> : expr<tensor<N>, N>; tensor.hpp:236 struct tensor<d0, d1, ds...> { (no base); h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:990 struct alignas(chunk_alignment) aligned_vector : expr<aligned_vector<N>, N>. So an implementer has precedent for both choices. 3. The choice has the consequences the finding names. h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:272-275 tensor(expr<B, d0, d1, ds...> const& rhs) and h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:70 dmatensor(expr<B, R, C, Ds...> const& other) need a derived-to-base conversion, so tensor<64,128> t(owner) and dmatensor(owner) compile only if the owner derives from expr. h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:147-150 operator()(expr<B, in_d0, in_d1> const& v_in, ...) with :168-169 "It's critical that v_in be captured by reference here" is viable for an owner only if the owner derives from expr. The design's sentence "Deleting only as_view() && is insufficient. The existing general expression slice accepts a const reference" gives that overload as its reason, so the sentence presupposes owner : expr without saying so. 4. Intent check (Ben #2/#3, jhan's prompt): Ben's straw-man owner class alignas(64) vnni_tensor has no base class and only as_view() overloads; Ben's #2 asks for "the usual operations that we expect of tensor types" following "tensor and dtensor which provide a uniform interface based upon expr". Both readings are compatible with Ben; the finding's recommendation (inherit expr, forward to as_view()) leans toward Ben's #2 precedent and the finding explicitly offers the alternative ("If the owner is not an expr, delete the 'insufficient' sentence"). jhan's prompt does not constrain this. So the finding does not push the design away from stated intent, and it is not mere taste: the choice decides whether owners can be passed directly to tensor/dtensor constructors and whether a deleted rvalue slice overload is required. One correction to the finding's parenthetical (see correction field): the Owner& overload at slice.hpp:38-42 CAN bind a const rvalue owner (Owner deduced as const vnni_tensor<R,D>, const& binds a temporary, and inside the overload owner is an lvalue so the const& as_view() is chosen and the deleted && overloads never fire). So the "insufficient" claim is true even without an expr base for that corner case, but the reason the design gives for it is only valid with an expr base. Analysis is by reading the overloads, not by compiling.
    Correction: The finding's parenthetical "otherwise the Owner& slice overload cannot bind an rvalue and the hazard does not exist" is over-stated. It is true for non-const rvalue owners. For a const rvalue owner (for example a function returning const vnni_tensor<R,D> by value), slice.hpp:38-42 deduces Owner = const vnni_tensor<R,D>, binds the temporary to const vnni_tensor&, calls the as_view() const& overload on the now-lvalue parameter, and returns a view into the temporary. So "Deleting only as_view() && is insufficient" is true in both cases; what depends on the owner being an expr is the design's stated REASON (the expr<B,in_d0,in_d1> const& slice overload). Corrected recommendation: state the owner's base class explicitly; if it inherits expr, keep the sentence and add deleted vnni_tensor&& and const vnni_tensor&& slice overloads; if it does not, replace the reason with the const-rvalue case or require callers to write owner.as_view().

Back to the finding

Minor F35. Row chunk/set mechanics and override signature unspecified

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). All five evidence citations hold at f46e48bab498. chunky<bf16,true>::set writes 16 contiguous bf16 at data + i*chunk_size [h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:49-59]. The rank-1 view uses the chunky vector path only when s0 == 1; otherwise chunk() is a scalar gather and set() is a scalar store loop [h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:155-166, 186-204]. scaled_v_expr::odd_part is and hi_16_of_32 (0xffff0000, ctor line 2077) and even_part is slli_epi32 16, both cast to fp32x16 [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2000-2007]. The odd-token bf16 set_v path is cvtepu16_epi32, slli 16, load pair, ternarylogic, store [kv_cache.hpp:2305-2318]. The expr base detects an override by if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk) [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:110-117]. The stride-2 rank-1 view is a real type: operator[] returns *(data + i*s0) with s0 a template parameter [view.hpp:146-148]. Two parts of the claim overreach. First, the C++ failure mode is misstated. If vnni_row declares a bf16_chunk whose parameter type, return type, or const-qualifier differs from the base, the two pointer-to-member types have no composite pointer type, so the comparison in the if-constexpr condition is ill-formed and the build fails; it does not fall back silently. A difference only in noexcept still forms a composite pointer type and the override is detected. The silent fp32 round trip happens only when vnni_row declares no member named bf16_chunk at all, which is how the rank-1 view behaves today (view.hpp has no bf16_chunk). The codebase convention is bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept [h/tron/hardware/permuted.hpp:53; expr.hpp:322, 715], and dmatensor.hpp:367 shows a requires-constrained version also works, so "non-template with that exact signature" is stricter than the code needs. Second, the ambiguity is narrower than claimed. The design already gives the address formula (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dimension + token%2 (section 4), says vnni_row stores the pair's first address plus its even/odd parity (section 5), requires vector extraction for chunk(i) (section 5, which excludes the scalar stride-2 view by requirement, though not by name), describes the odd write as read the pair, change one row, write the pair back (section 6), and tells the implementer to move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code without scalar loops (step 2, with S5 pointing at set_v). An implementer following these cannot build the wrong thing; several equivalent intrinsic sequences (ternarylogic or mask_blend_epi16) satisfy the design. What the design does not say, and the finding correctly notes, is that even_part and odd_part are private members of scaled_v_expr [kv_cache.hpp:1996], so they must be moved or duplicated, not called, and that one row chunk equals one 64-byte pair load at pair_base + 32*i followed by a parity half-select.
    Correction: Ambiguity (narrow): the design specifies the packed-row mechanics at the level of formula, pair-base-plus-parity row state, read-modify-write for the partner row, and "move the existing set_v / scaled_v_expr code"; it omits only (a) the explicit statement that a row chunk is one 64-byte pair load at pair_base + 32*i followed by an even (slli 16) or odd (and 0xffff0000) half-select, and (b) that even_part/odd_part are private to scaled_v_expr [kv_cache.hpp:1996-2007@f46e48bab498] and so must be moved into the new header rather than called. On bf16_chunk: vnni_row must declare a member named bf16_chunk following the codebase convention bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept [permuted.hpp:53, expr.hpp:322/715, dmatensor.hpp:367@f46e48bab498]. Omitting the member entirely is the only way to get the silent fp32 round trip; a mismatched parameter, return type, or const-qualifier fails at the &B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk comparison [expr.hpp:112] as a compile error, and a missing noexcept is tolerated. Naming the stride-2 view alternative is optional: the design's "extract with vector operations" requirement already rules it out because its chunk() and set() are scalar when s0 != 1 [view.hpp:155-166, 186-204].
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Short version: two of the three parts of F35 are already covered by the design, the third (the bf16_chunk override detection) is a real omission, and F35 misstates its consequence. So the finding stands only in corrected form. Part A (chunk-to-pair mapping and even/odd merge "unspecified"): the design covers this at design level. It gives the address formula "(token / 2) * (2 * D) + 2 * dimension + token % 2" [design-text.txt:184], from which chunk i of token t is 32 contiguous bf16 at pair base + 32*i. It says vnni_row "stores the address of the first value in the token pair. It also stores whether its token is the even or odd member" [design-text.txt:242]. Step 2 says "Move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code. Preserve the single-pass even-token write. Preserve the odd-token operation that reads the pair, updates one row, and writes it back. Do not replace these vector operations with scalar loops" [design-text.txt:456]. Section 6 says ordinary row assignment "cannot use the current behavior of page::set_v(even), which clears the odd row" [design-text.txt:392]. Those sentences point the implementer at the exact primitives F35 lists (scaled_v_expr::odd_part/even_part at kv_cache.hpp:1999-2006@f46e48bab498, the set_v odd path ternarylogic merge at kv_cache.hpp:2305-2320@f46e48bab498). The instruction-level recipe F35 asks for is implementation detail, not a design gap. Part C (stride-2 view alternative not named): the design states the requirement that rules it out. "Extract the packed values with vector operations" [design-text.txt:258]; "Do not substitute: A loop that reads one scalar value at a time through the new row accessors" [design-text.txt:412]. I confirmed the alternative exists and is scalar: rank-1 view<T,aligned,dma,seq<d0>,sseq<s0>> with s0 != 1 gathers in chunk() [view.hpp:155-166@f46e48bab498] and stores scalar in set() [view.hpp:186-204@f46e48bab498], and the type is declared final [view.hpp:116@f46e48bab498] so it cannot be extended. F35 itself concedes the alternative is scalar, so it gives no evidence the design's choice is wrong. Naming the rejected alternative is a note, not a required change. Part B (bf16_chunk override detection): this is a real omission, but F35 overstates the consequence. Verified: expr<B,N>::bf16_chunk dispatches with "if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk)" [expr.hpp:109-116@f46e48bab498]. The two existing overriders both use the non-template form "bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t i) const noexcept" [dmatensor.hpp:367-371@f46e48bab498 (with a requires clause); permuted.hpp:53@f46e48bab498]. The design requires the override ("Extract the stored bf16 bits directly" [design-text.txt:260]) but says "Match the existing view definitions" [design-text.txt:246], and the existing rank-1 view defines no bf16_chunk at all [view.hpp:116-204@f46e48bab498], so that instruction does not lead the implementer to the override signature or to the detection mechanism. Correction to F35's consequence: the silent fallback happens only when vnni_row omits bf16_chunk entirely. A template or overloaded bf16_chunk makes "&B::bf16_chunk" ill-formed and the comparison fails to compile at the first use, which is loud, not silent. The silent fallback is bit-exact, not a numerics change: chunk(i) yields fp32 lanes with zero low 16 bits [bf16.hpp:45-49@f46e48bab498 and the even_part/odd_part forms], and fp32s_to_bf16s only alters bits when the low 16 bits are nonzero [bf16.hpp:92-109@f46e48bab498]. So the cost of omission is extra conversion instructions. The design's own checks would not catch it: "Copy bf16 rows without changing their bits" [design-text.txt:514] passes under the fallback, and the instruction-comparison list [design-text.txt:552] names the even write, odd write and weighted sum but not bf16 extraction. That is why Part B survives as a minor correction.
    Correction: Omission (minor): the design requires vnni_row to override bf16_chunk but does not say how the expr base detects an override. expr<B,N>::bf16_chunk compares member-function pointers ("&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk", h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:109-116@f46e48bab498). The design should say: declare exactly one non-template "bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept" in vnni_row (the form used by dmatensor.hpp:367 and permuted.hpp:53 at f46e48bab498). If the override is omitted, the base silently falls back to fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)). That fallback is bit-exact for stored bf16 values (the low 16 bits are zero after widening, so the NaN correction in fp32s_to_bf16s never fires), so the design's "Copy bf16 rows without changing their bits" check does not detect the omission. Add bf16 extraction (bf16_chunk) to the generated-instruction comparison list in "Performance acceptance". A template or overloaded bf16_chunk fails to compile at first use; that case is loud and needs no design text. The chunk-to-pair mapping, the read-modify-write merge, and the rejection of scalar strided access are already stated in the design (formula in section 4, vnni_row description and operations table in section 5, step 2, section 6) and need no additional mechanics paragraph. Naming the rejected stride-2 view alternative is optional (note level).
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). All five evidence citations hold at f46e48bab498. chunky.hpp:50-59: chunky<bf16,true>::set writes 16 contiguous bf16 via tron_mm_store_bf16(data + i*chunk_size). view.hpp:155-166 and 186-204: rank-1 view chunk() falls to a scalar gather and set() to a scalar store whenever s0 != 1, so a stride-2 view of a packed row is scalar. kv_cache.hpp:1998-2006 (finding says 2000-2007, off by two lines): scaled_v_expr::odd_part = and hi_16_of_32, even_part = slli_epi32 16, both cast to fp32x16. kv_cache.hpp:2306-2318: the odd set_v path is cvtepu16_epi32, slli 16, load_si512(v_ptr + i*2), ternarylogic, store_si512. expr.hpp:111-117: if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk) detects the override by member-pointer comparison. The design text (design-text.txt:258-262, 456) says only "Extract the packed values with vector operations", "Preserve the other row in its stored pair", and "Move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code"; the S5 link points at set_v L2289-L2398. It never says that chunk i of a packed row is one 64-byte load at pair_base + 32*i followed by one shift or one and, and it never mentions the bf16_chunk override mechanism. So the ambiguity and the omission are real. Intent check: Ben's comment #3 asks for "the existing interleave/deinterleave operations" in per-layout overloads "rather than a generic scalar loop in the hot path"; the finding asks the design to name exactly those mechanics, so it moves toward Ben's intent, not away. One correction to the finding: the primitives closest to chunk()/bf16_chunk() are page::get_v(float*) at kv_cache.hpp:2408-2432 (load_si512(v_ptr + i*2), slli 16 for even, and 0xffff0000 for odd) and get_v(bf16*) at 2440-2464 (cvtepi32_epi16 for even, cvtepi32_epi16(srli 16) for odd), which the design's step 2 "unpacking" already alludes to without lines; and the "silently falls back" clause is overstated (see correction). Severity minor: the design states the vector requirement and links the source, so the implementer would not build the wrong thing, but the text should add the mechanics and the override rule. Confidence high.
    Correction: The claim "vnni_row must declare a non-template bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept with that exact signature or the base silently falls back to fp32 conversion" is partly wrong. Silent fallback happens only when vnni_row declares no bf16_chunk at all. A member template, an overload set, or a parameter type other than size_t makes &B::bf16_chunk ill-formed in the if-constexpr condition, which is a compile error, not a silent fallback; a missing noexcept still compares as different and the override is used. The fallback itself, fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)) (simd/bf16.hpp:92-109), is bit-exact for data that came from bf16: bf16 to fp32 is a 16-bit shift, and the conversion back truncates and only sets 0x00010000 when the exponent is saturated and the low 16 bits are nonzero, which never holds for bf16-origin values. So the consequence of a missed override is the extra convert cost that the design's row "Avoid a float conversion during exact copies" forbids, not a change of stored bits. Corrected guidance: declare a non-template, non-overloaded bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept on vnni_row, matching the existing overrides at hardware/permuted.hpp:53 and hardware/dmatensor.hpp:367; if it is omitted the copy compiles and stays bit-exact but pays a float round trip. Also name page::get_v(float*) and get_v(bf16*) (kv_cache.hpp:2408-2464) as the existing vector read of one packed row, since they are the direct template for chunk() and bf16_chunk(); scaled_v_expr::even_part/odd_part are the same two instructions inside the weighted-sum path.

Minor F37. No-read pair store conflicts with odd-boundary writable slices

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding holds. (1) The design quotes are verbatim: "matrix.set(expr) | Initialize complete pairs without reading old destination bytes" [design-text.txt:264]; "If a slice starts at an odd token, keep that token in the odd position of its stored pair" [284]; "The starting row may be odd" [286]; "Keep writable slices writable" [288]. (2) The packed V formula "(token / 2) * (2 * D) + 2 * dimension + token % 2" [design-text.txt:184] puts tokens 2p and 2p+1 in one stored pair unit, so a slice that starts at 2p+1 or ends at 2q shares its boundary pair with a row outside the slice; a no-read pair store on that pair overwrites the outside row. (3) The cited precedent is accurate: h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1866-1920@f46e48bab498 append_v even-destination paths store "[0 b]"/"[0 a]" with no destination load, odd-destination paths load the destination pair and merge with _mm512_ternarylogic_epi32. (4) The existing slice<> returns a view (h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:57-59@f46e48bab498) and the existing rank-2 view::set(expr<X,d0,d1,...>) iterates rows and calls the row set (h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:87-91@f46e48bab498). "Keep the existing form of the function calls" therefore implies a packed slice supports .set(matrix_expr), and the design never states whether that call uses the row-by-row (pair-preserving) path or the no-read pair store it defines for the full matrix. The full-matrix rule is safe only because the matrix "requires a positive even number of rows" [design-text.txt:190]; the slice is "a small internal slice expression" [288], a different type with no stated .set semantics. The finding omits one mitigating fact: the verification table already requires "Read and write a slice beginning at an odd token ... Check the coordinates and unchanged neighboring values" [design-text.txt:522]. That row states the required outcome but not the write path, so a test written through slice[row].set() would pass while slice.set(matrix_expr) still used the no-read pair store. The gap in section 5 is real and the one-sentence recommendation closes it.
    Correction: Ambiguity confirmed, with one addition: the design's verification table already requires unchanged neighboring values after writing a slice that begins at an odd token [design-text.txt:522], so the required outcome is stated. What is missing is the interface-level statement in section 5 of which write path slice-level .set(matrix_expr) uses (row-by-row pair-preserving, or the no-read pair store only for pairs whose both rows lie inside the slice). The test row does not pin the write path, so it would not catch a slice.set(matrix_expr) built on the no-read store if the test writes through the row path.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's source evidence holds: append_v at main f46e48ba [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1866-1920] has two even-destination branches that store the pair with the partner zeroed and no destination read ("src=[a b]; store [0 b]"), and two odd-destination branches that load the destination pair and merge. Those are the only existing pair-write precedents. The existing slice of a view returns a view that carries set() [h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:41-79 @f46e48ba], so an implementer following precedent would give the packed slice a matrix-level set() and must choose a write path for boundary pairs. I read the whole design text and grepped every sentence in the HTML containing slice, set(, full-matrix, complete pairs, or neighbor. Section 5 says "matrix.set(expr) | Initialize complete pairs without reading old destination bytes", "If a slice starts at an odd token, keep that token in the odd position of its stored pair", and "Keep writable slices writable", but no sentence in section 5 says which path a slice-level write uses at a partial pair. The design does partly cover the concern elsewhere: section 6 says "Full-matrix assignment must initialize complete pairs without reading destination bytes" (the word Full-matrix scopes the no-read rule to the whole matrix), and the section 8 verification row says "Read and write a slice beginning at an odd token. ... Check the coordinates and unchanged neighboring values", which would fail if a slice write erased the even neighbor. That coverage is partial: it lives in the acceptance test and a scoping adjective, not in the interface specification, and the test names only a slice that begins at an odd token, not one that ends at an even token (odd row count), which has a trailing partial pair. So the ambiguity in section 5 stands, with reduced risk because the design's own acceptance test would catch the leading-boundary error.
    Correction: The design already implies the rule in two places: section 6 scopes the no-read pair store to "Full-matrix assignment", and the section 8 verification row requires "unchanged neighboring values" after writing a slice that begins at an odd token. The interface section 5 still does not state it, and no sentence covers a slice whose last row is the even half of a pair (odd row count). Add to the slice paragraph in section 5: "A slice write uses the pair-preserving row path for any pair that the slice covers only partly (first row odd, or last row even). The no-read pair store applies only to the full matrix and to pairs whose both rows lie inside the slice." Also extend the section 8 slice test to include a slice with an odd row count that starts at an even token, and check the untouched odd neighbor after the write.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's evidence holds. (1) The design quotes are verbatim: "matrix.set(expr) | Initialize complete pairs without reading old destination bytes" (design-text.txt line 264), "If a slice starts at an odd token, keep that token in the odd position of its stored pair" (line 284), "The starting row may be odd" (line 286), "Keep writable slices writable" (line 288). (2) The packed V formula (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dimension + token%2 (line 184) means tokens 2p and 2p+1 share every 64-byte chunk of pair p, so a no-read pair store on a boundary pair of a slice that starts or ends mid-pair overwrites the neighbor row outside the slice. (3) h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1866-1922@f46e48bab498 (append_v under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE==16) is the only existing pair-write code: even-destination paths store [0 b]/[0 a] without reading dst, odd-destination paths load dst and ternarylogic-merge; the comment there says the zeroing is safe only because destinations are fresh ranges. (4) The design nowhere defines a slice-level set operation or which path it uses: every slice mention (lines 51, 274, 282-306, 454, 522) says only "writable" and "read-only"; section 6 (lines 392, 430) distinguishes "ordinary row assignment" (pair-preserving) from "full-matrix assignment" (no-read pair store) and a slice is neither. The existing rank-2 view::set at h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:85-91@f46e48bab498 is row-by-row, but the design deliberately departs from that for matrix.set, so an implementer cannot infer the slice rule from precedent. INTENT check: Ben's comment #2 explicitly wants to "convert/slice/compute them freely", so slices are in scope, and Ben's #3 says the class "must not remove V's zero-initialization invariant or add implicit clearing"; the recommended clarification (slice writes take the pair-preserving row path, no-read pair stores only for the full matrix or pairs fully inside the slice) keeps both, so it does not push the design away from Ben's or jhan's intent. It is not mere taste: an implementer who models slice.set on matrix.set would erase a neighbor row at an odd boundary. Partial mitigation: the design's own verification row (line 522, "Read and write a slice beginning at an odd token ... Check the coordinates and unchanged neighboring values") encodes the behavioral requirement, so a wrong implementation would fail the design's test rather than merge silently. That keeps the severity at minor.
    Correction: The finding is right that section 5 never states which write path a slice uses, but it overstates the gap slightly: section 8's slice test ("Read and write a slice beginning at an odd token ... Check the coordinates and unchanged neighboring values", design-text.txt line 522) already requires neighbor preservation as an acceptance criterion. The missing text is the mechanism in section 5: whether the slice expression exposes a matrix-level set at all, and if so that it must write row by row through the pair-preserving path (or use no-read pair stores only for pairs whose both rows lie inside the slice), never the full-matrix no-read store on a boundary pair.
