Minor F38. Load side unnamed; Ben's store_row/load_row not mapped

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The omission holds. Checked at f46e48bab498: (1) The design's interface table (design-text lines 254-274) lists matrix[token][dim], chunk(i), bf16_chunk(i), row.set, matrix.set, view assignment, as_view, copy/move, ctor/assign, const conversion; the "Separate adding cache rows" block names only append_v_row and set_pair (store side). No bulk row-read operation is named anywhere. Section 6 line 444 says "Unpack packed V into the existing temporary buffer supplied by the caller" and step 7 (line 466) says "logical row extraction operations" without naming one. (2) Ben's comment 5765866077 section 4 declares load_row(vnni_view<const bf16, k_vnni_layout<Tokens, Dims>> src, size_t token, std::span<bf16, Dims> dst) and adds "Corresponding overloads for v_vnni_layout" and "For V, the overloads contain the existing interleave/deinterleave operations" (evidence/github-review.json, verbatim). (3) get_v bodies are at kv_cache.hpp:2408-2441 (float*) and 2442-2472 (bf16*), each for (i = 0; i < geometry.head_size; i += 16) { _mm512_load_si512(v_ptr + i * 2); ... }. gof.cpp:213 is verbatim v_heads[h] = pi.v_head_fn(slot, h, v_scratch.data() + h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE); and full.hpp:2652-2657 wraps get_v<hardware_attention_geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, off, dest). (4) Strengthening evidence the finding did not cite: the existing view<bf16>::set(expr) [h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:187-191] pulls fp32 x.chunk(i) and stores through chunky<bf16,true>::set [h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:55-57], which applies fp32s_to_bf16s; it never calls bf16_chunk(). So the tron-idiomatic bulk read dst_row_view.set(v[token]) would take the fp32 round trip (slli 16 in chunk, then srli 16 plus NaN check), not today's 3-instruction get_v(bf16*) sequence (load, cvtepi32_epi16, store). The design says "Prefer bf16_chunk() to preserve the bits of bf16 sources" (line 410) but names no operation that consumes bf16_chunk(), so the implementer's choice is real and not purely cosmetic. Partial counter-evidence: design line 346 says "Keep existing page methods where needed. Make them call the new logical operations", which lets get_v survive as the bulk read, but the "new logical operations" list still has no bulk read for it to call.
    Correction: Two details need correcting; the core claim stands. (a) The finding says get_v is "one 4-iteration AVX-512 loop". The loop steps 16 dimensions per iteration [kv_cache.hpp:2411,2422,2445,2456 at f46e48bab498], so at head_size 128 (the GOF path's HEAD_SIZE_128 geometry) it runs 8 iterations, which matches the finding's own "eight bf16_chunk() calls"; 4 iterations holds only for head_size 64. (b) The finding's recommendation says "keep the float destination overload only if a caller of get_v(float*) remains": at f46e48bab498 the only production caller of get_v is full.hpp:2654 with a bf16* destination; get_v(float*) has four test callers in t/t_llama_unit.cpp (lines 1905, 2209 via tensor<head_size>.data.data(), 2431, 2494), so the float overload cannot simply be dropped without updating those tests. Also worth adding to the claim: the existing view<bf16>::set(expr) path [view.hpp:187-191, chunky.hpp:55-57] does not call bf16_chunk(), so the design's "Prefer bf16_chunk()" guidance for exact copies has no named consumer unless a bulk read (get_v body or a load_row) is specified.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Evidence checked. (1) The finding's citations hold at f46e48bab498: get_v bodies at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2408 (float* destination) and :2442 (bf16* destination), each a for (i = 0; i < head_size; i += 16) loop doing one _mm512_load_si512 per 16 dimensions; src/tron/gof.cpp:213 reads verbatim v_heads[h] = pi.v_head_fn(slot, h, v_scratch.data() + h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE); inside the per-token, per-head populate loop (gof.cpp:203-213); h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:2652-2657 defines v_head_fn as a call to get_v into the caller's dest. Ben's comment 5765866077 section 4 names load_row(vnni_view<const bf16, k_vnni_layout<Tokens, Dims>> src, size_t token, std::span<bf16, Dims> dst) next to store_row and says "Corresponding overloads for v_vnni_layout" and "For V, the overloads contain the existing interleave/deinterleave operations". (2) Design-text fairness: the HTML contains 0 occurrences of load_row, store_row or store_block, so Ben's three named bulk operations are never mapped; the section 2 table only says "Preserve the optimized operations". The store side gets names and signatures ("append_v_row(destination_view, token, source_row_view)", "destination_view.set_pair(...)"), the load side gets none. (3) However, the design does cover the load-side BEHAVIOR in four places the finding does not credit: step 2 "Move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code ... Do not replace these vector operations with scalar loops"; step 7 "Use typed append, copy, and logical row extraction operations ... Keep the parent PR's GOF callback interfaces"; section 6 "Unpack packed V into the existing temporary buffer supplied by the caller"; section 5 "Keep existing page methods where needed. Make them call the new logical operations"; the caller checklist "Check set_v and get_v, which write and read V rows." So the implementer IS told to keep get_v's vector loop and move it under the typed layer; the finding's "cannot tell whether to keep that loop" overstates the gap. (4) The alternative the finding poses ("rebuild the row from eight bf16_chunk() calls") is not what existing assignment machinery would do: view::set(expr) at h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:188-191 calls x.chunk(i) (fp32) and chunky<bf16,true>::set (h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:55-58) re-narrows via fp32s_to_bf16s, so a native-row-view .set(packed_row) would add a widen/narrow pair per chunk instead of today's single _mm512_cvtepi32_epi16. That makes the design's evident intent (a dedicated moved routine) the only route that preserves the instruction sequence, which strengthens the case for naming it. Coverage is partial (behavior specified, name/signature and Ben-mapping missing), so per the lens: not refuted, correction written. (5) Factual slip inside the finding: "one 4-iteration AVX-512 loop" is wrong for the AMX kernel shape; at head_size 128 the i += 16 loop runs 8 iterations (4 only at head_size 64). The finding's own next sentence ("eight bf16_chunk() calls") already assumes 8.
    Correction: Omission (narrower than stated): the design already specifies the load-side behavior. Step 2 says to move the existing V unpacking code into the typed operation layer and forbids scalar loops. Step 7 says hardware preparation (full.hpp) must use "logical row extraction operations". Section 6 says to unpack packed V into the caller's existing temporary buffer. Section 5 says existing page methods such as get_v may stay and call the new logical operations. What is missing is only (a) a name and signature for that operation, matching the store side's append_v_row/set_pair, and (b) an explicit mapping of Ben's store_row, load_row and store_block to the design's operations (those three names appear 0 times in the page). The recommendation stands: add load_row(vnni_view<const bf16,R,D> src, size_t token, view<bf16,true,false,seq<D>> dst) (and a float-destination overload only if a get_v(float*) caller remains) to the section 5 list, state it is today's get_v loop [kv_cache.hpp:2408-2470] moved, and add a three-row mapping table (Ben store_row -> append_v_row + set_pair and row.set; Ben load_row -> load_row; Ben store_block -> child PR only). Also note that the existing view::set(expr) route [view.hpp:188-191, chunky.hpp:55-58] widens to fp32 and re-narrows, so it would NOT reproduce get_v's single cvtepi32_epi16 per chunk; this is a reason to name the dedicated routine rather than rely on generic assignment. Fix the finding's "4-iteration" to "8 iterations at head_size 128 (one 512-bit load per 16 dimensions)".
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The omission is real and it is aligned with what Ben asked for, so it does not push the design away from his intent. Ben's review submission #2 asks for "the row-wise load/store operations that we need here" and his straw-man (#3, section 4) names load_row next to store_row (verbatim in evidence/github-review.json). The design names the store side (append_v_row, set_pair, row.set) but never names the load side: the only load-direction text is "Unpack packed V into the existing temporary buffer supplied by the caller" (section 6), "Move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code" (step 2) and "logical row extraction operations" (step 7); grep of status/design-new-tensor-type.html finds no load_row and no named unpack operation. The cited source facts hold at f46e48bab498: get_v(float*) and get_v(bf16*) bodies are at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2408-2465 (per-16-dimension loop: _mm512_load_si512 then slli/and for float, cvtepi32_epi16 for bf16); src/tron/gof.cpp:213 calls pi.v_head_fn(slot, h, v_scratch.data() + h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE) once per token per head; h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:2653-2656 implements v_head_fn by calling get_v into dest. The consequence is concrete, not taste: the tron-idiomatic alternative dst_view.set(v[token]) goes through view::set at h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:187-191, which calls x.chunk(i) (fp32) and chunky<bf16,true>::set, which converts with fp32s_to_bf16s (h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:50-60). That reproduces the bits (low 16 bits are zero, so no NaN quieting) but not get_v(bf16*)'s load + cvtepi32_epi16 + store sequence. The design's performance-acceptance list compares generated instructions only for "the combined even-row write and partner clear, the odd-row write, and the weighted V sum" (section 8); the row read is missing from that list, so the design would not catch the slower unpack. Two details in the finding need correcting (below), but they do not weaken the claim.
    Correction: Corrected claim: the design names no load-side bulk operation (Ben's load_row), and its instruction-comparison list omits the row read, so an implementer who writes the FPGA-staging unpack as dst.set(v[token]) gets a fp32 round trip (view.hpp:187-191 + chunky.hpp:50-60 at f46e48bab498) instead of get_v(bf16*)'s cvtepi32_epi16 sequence (kv_cache.hpp:2447-2465). Fix: name the load operation (or state that the bf16 unpack must be built on bf16_chunk(i), whose body is one get_v iteration) and add the row read to the section 8 instruction comparison. Detail corrections to the finding text: (1) "one 4-iteration AVX-512 loop" is wrong for the staging geometry; the loop runs head_size/16 iterations and HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE = 128 (h/libpos.hpp:72), so 8 iterations, matching the finding's own "eight bf16_chunk() calls". (2) get_v(float*) has no production caller at main; only t/t_llama_unit.cpp uses it (lines 1904-1905, 2208-2210, 2430-2432, 2493-2494), production uses the bf16* form (full.hpp:2653-2656); the design's "Keep existing page methods where needed" already covers keeping it as a page wrapper over row.chunk(i). (3) The recommendation's v_vnni_view is not a design type; the design's type is vnni_view<T, R, D>.

Minor F39. page::k_tensor duplicates page::k in the parent

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Core claim verified from primary sources; it holds. (1) page::k returns the same view the design assigns to k_tensor. At f46e48bab498, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp lines 1547-1570: every page::k(slot, kv_head) overload (uniform and <geometry>, mutable and const) returns kv_block(...).k_view(). Lines 2208-2220: kv_block::k_view() returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> (const form: const_view of the same shape) via reinterpret_cast of the k array. The design's single mention of k_tensor (grep of the HTML: exactly 1 occurrence, section 5 "Page and kernel arguments that identify the layout") says verbatim: "Add page::k_tensor<geometry>(slot, head) to provide a matrix view of K without copying its values. Native K returns its existing row-major view." and two sentences later "Keep existing page methods where needed." No other rationale, contract, or migration instruction for k_tensor appears anywhere in the page. So in the parent PR, k_tensor and page::k return identical types from identical storage; the finding's "rename with no behavior" reading is what the text says. (2) The design's own typed kernel boundary does not need k_tensor: it declares qk_rowmajor_128x4(const_native_k_view<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> k, ...) with const_native_k_view<Rows,Cols> = const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<Rows,Cols>> (design section 5, "Owner, matrix view, and row expression" block). That is exactly the type page::k<geometry>(slot, kv_head) const already returns at f46e48ba. Existing callers at main (model.hpp:2757 pg.template k<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(kout); self_attention.hpp:1423, 1545, 1580) can pass page::k unchanged. (3) Child evidence verified: git diff f46e48bab498 30c4ac82cbb6 -- h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp shows all 8 page::k overloads gain !k_vnni::layout_on<head_size> / !k_vnni::layout_on<geometry.head_size> in their requires clauses, and adds k_vnni_plane<geometry>() (const bf16*, requires layout_on), set_k_row, get_k_row, set_k_block. Child call sites (model.hpp/self_attention.hpp/full.hpp diff) use set_k_row/set_k_block/k_vnni_plane/get_k_row, not a renamed page::k. (4) Ben #3 (github-review.json body, section 5) verbatim: using k_storage = std::conditional_t<k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, vnni_tensor<k_vnni_layout<page_size, head_size>>, existing_row_major_k_storage>; k_storage k; inside kv_block, plus a page accessor k_packed(...) returning kv_block<geometry>(slot, head).k.as_view(). Ben selects the K type at the storage level and adds a distinct accessor only for the packed case; he does not rename the native-K accessor. This matches the finding's recommendation (keep page::k, no alias in the parent). (5) Recommendation's premise about page::v verified: at f46e48ba kv_cache.hpp lines 1674-1720, page::v(slot, kv_head, i_page) exists only under #else // TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 and returns a rank-1 row view view<bf16,true,false,seq<head_size>>; the CHUNK 16 branch has set_v/get_v/v_data and no page::v. So "v_tensor" as a distinct name for a matrix accessor is justified, and the asymmetry (add v_tensor, skip k_tensor) is sound. Not refuted. Kind (b) design disagreement, minor severity, is the right classification: the parent is not wrong to compile with an alias, but the design gives no reason for it and Ben's precedent does not include it.
    Correction: Two sub-points need adjusting, without changing the verdict. (a) The sentence "the child would then have to constrain two K accessors instead of one for VNNI slots" overstates the cost. With a parent-side k_tensor alias, the child must constrain page::k (8 overloads, as 30c4ac82cbb6 already does) and turn k_tensor into the layout-selecting accessor. Without the alias, the child must constrain page::k and add a layout-selecting accessor. The child work is the same in both cases; the objection is only that the parent carries a purposeless second name, not that the child does extra work. (b) The recommendation "Move 'layout-selected K accessor' to the 'Then update PR #4424' list" is partly already satisfied: that list already contains "Select the K owner and view using the existing conditions on storage geometry." The actionable change is only to delete the k_tensor sentence from section 5 (or, if the author intends k_tensor as a stable call-site seam so the child changes only the accessor body, state that rationale and instruct the parent to migrate the page::k callers at model.hpp:2757 and self_attention.hpp:1423/1545/1580 to it; the current text does neither).
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). I read the whole design text and grepped the HTML for every mention of k_tensor. The name appears once (design-new-tensor-type.html line 245: "Add page::k_tensor<geometry>(slot, head) to provide a matrix view of K without copying its values. Native K returns its existing row-major view."). No other sentence gives a reason for a second K accessor in the parent, defers it, or flags it as deliberate. The nearest sentences are "Keep existing page methods where needed. Make them call the new logical operations." (line 247, which keeps page::k, so both names would exist) and, in the child list, "Select the K owner and view using the existing conditions on storage geometry." (design-text.txt line 492, which does not name k_tensor). So the design does not cover the criticism. The finding's evidence holds in the source: page::k<geometry>(slot, kv_head) at f46e48bab498 h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1557-1570 returns kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k_view(), and k_view() at kv_cache.hpp:2208-2219 returns view<bf16,true,false,seq<page_size,head_size>> (const_view for the const overload). That is byte-for-byte the design's own native_k_view / const_native_k_view alias (design-text.txt lines 230-236), so in the parent k_tensor would return the same type page::k already returns. The child diff f46e48bab498..30c4ac82cbb6 for kv_cache.hpp adds !k_vnni::layout_on<...> to all eight page::k overloads and adds k_vnni_plane under layout_on, as the finding says. Ben's #3 section 5 (evidence/github-review.json) selects k_storage inside kv_block with std::conditional_t and adds an accessor (k_packed) only for the packed case; it adds no new accessor for native K. The K callers at main (self_attention.hpp:1423,1545,1580; model.hpp:2757; full.hpp:2647) already receive a typed view from page::k, so the parent's definition-of-done sentence "pass views whose types identify the cache layout" is met for K by page::k alone.
    Correction: The main claim stands: in the parent, page::k_tensor is a second name for the type page::k already returns, and the design gives no reason for it. One sub-claim is overstated: "the child would then have to constrain two K accessors instead of one." Under either approach the child does the same work. It constrains the eight page::k overloads with !k_vnni::layout_on (as 30c4ac82cbb6 already does), and it has one layout-selected K accessor (k_tensor re-typed under the design, or a new accessor under the finding's recommendation). So the argument for dropping k_tensor from the parent is only "no parent behavior and no stated rationale," not extra child work. The design should either drop page::k_tensor from the parent (finding's recommendation) or add one sentence stating why it exists (for example: the child re-types this one accessor by layout while page::k stays row-major only, and its name pairs with v_tensor). Without that sentence an implementing agent must guess whether callers migrate from page::k to k_tensor.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The evidence holds. At main f46e48bab498, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1557-1570 page::k<geometry>(slot, kv_head) returns kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k_view(), and kv_cache.hpp:2208-2219 k_view() returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> (const_view for const). The design's native_k_view alias (design-text lines 231-236) is that same type, so "Native K returns its existing row-major view" (design-text line 342) makes page::k_tensor a second name for exactly what page::k already returns. The design also says "Keep existing page methods where needed" (line 346), so both names coexist in the parent with no behavior difference. Line 342 is the only mention of k_tensor; the design never says what it returns for VNNI slots in the child, so the implementer must guess. Intent check: Ben #3 section 5 puts the K layout selection in kv_block ("using k_storage = std::conditional_t<k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, vnni_tensor<...>, existing_row_major_k_storage>") and names the VNNI accessor k_packed; he asks for no renamed native-K accessor, and native K stays "existing_row_major_k_storage". Ben #1 ("Why do we need to introduce a new function for this?") shows he questions duplicate functions. jhan asked that the VNNI-K change be "a local change within the new tensor data type" in the child; the finding's recommendation keeps layout selection in the child, so it does not move away from jhan's intent. Child diff f46e48ba..30c4ac82cb confirms every page::k overload gains !k_vnni::layout_on and k_vnni_plane is added. One overstatement in the finding: the child would not need to "constrain two K accessors"; the natural reading is that k_tensor becomes the layout-selecting accessor (no constraint) while page::k keeps the !layout_on constraint it already has. The real cost is a redundant parent accessor plus a no-behavior caller migration.
    Correction: page::k_tensor in the parent returns the same type as page::k (confirmed), so it is a rename without behavior. The cost claim should read: the parent adds a redundant accessor and migrates callers with no behavior change, and the design leaves unstated what k_tensor returns for VNNI slots in the child (an ambiguity), rather than "the child would have to constrain two K accessors". The recommendation (keep page::k, add only page::v_tensor, move the layout-selected K accessor to the child list) stays consistent with Ben #3 section 5 and jhan's "local change in the child" request.

Minor F40. std::start_lifetime_as not implemented in pinned toolchain

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Core claim holds: the pinned toolchain cannot provide std::start_lifetime_as (the C++23 library function that starts an object's lifetime in existing storage), so the design's sentence "A supported C++23 function for starting object lifetimes is another option" offers an option that will not compile. Toolchain pin, checked at both the design's main snapshot and the live main tip: - flake.nix:587@f46e48bab498 llvm19 = pkgs.llvmPackages_19; and flake.nix:1107 llvmStdenv = llvm19.stdenv; (Clang 19). Unchanged at 98bb8cb22f94 (same line numbers). - CMakeLists.txt:56@f46e48bab498 set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 23); unchanged at 98bb8cb2. - flake.nix:30 nixpkgs.follows = "haskellNix/nixpkgs-unstable"; flake.lock pins nixpkgs-unstable to c1cb7d097cb250f6e1904aacd5f2ba5ffd8a49ce (lastModified 2025-12-01) at BOTH f46e48ba and 98bb8cb2. In that nixpkgs revision, pkgs/top-level/all-packages.nix line 4578 reads gcc_latest = gcc15; (fetched via gh blob API eb80334a36b6abaa741697e66d1b1b99e31b48ec). So the libstdc++ behind llvmPackages_19.stdenv comes from GCC 15 or older. - The build uses GNU libstdc++, not libc++: t/CMakeLists.txt:608-612@f46e48ba "# _GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS: libstdc++ precondition checks ... The first test case needs them" (that macro is a no-op under libc++); CMakeLists.txt:837 deb dependency libstdc++6; flake.nix:546-549 "Linux only; macOS uses libc++". Library status, checked in the library sources themselves: - GCC 14 branch libstdc++-v3/include/std/memory: no occurrence of start_lifetime_as or __cpp_lib_start_lifetime_as. - GCC 15 branch libstdc++-v3/include/bits/version.def: no start_lifetime_as feature-test entry (entries after to_underlying are tuple_like, unreachable, algorithm_default_value_type). - cppreference compiler_support/23, row "Explicit lifetime management" (P2590R2): GCC libstdc++ = 16, Clang libc++ = blank, MSVC STL = 19.51. libcxx.llvm.org/Status/Cxx23.html shows P2590R2 with a blank status. The finding's "libc++ through LLVM 20" is therefore conservative; libc++ has no release with it at all. - The repository uses the function nowhere: git grep start_lifetime_as f46e48bab498 returns nothing. C++-semantics part of the claim, checked in tron source at f46e48bab498: - kv_cache.hpp:1202-1203 allocates the arena with hardware::try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite<uint8_t[]>; h/system/memory.hpp:323-339 calls dma_allocate_aligned and runs placement new only if constexpr (not std::is_trivially_constructible_v<U>), so for uint8_t no object is constructed. - kv_cache.hpp:1289, 1298, 1333 reinterpret_cast<kv_block_t*>(...) with no construction, as the finding says. - kv_cache.hpp:2191-2242 struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block has only public data members and member functions, no user-declared constructor or destructor, so it is an aggregate and an implicit-lifetime class, as the finding says. - src/pos/fake.cpp:352-356 dma_allocate_aligned = aligned_alloc(alignment, size) (on the implicit-object-creation list, [c.malloc]/4); src/system/memory.cpp:460-469 production dma_allocate_aligned = heap->allocate(sz, alignment) or v2_heap ... allocate (custom heap, not on that list). Matches the finding. Line numbers in the finding are off by one (353-357, 461) but point at the right functions. - Ben #3 verbatim in evidence/github-review.json: "The page arena must construct the actual storage type. We should not obtain 'typed storage' by casting an existing SIMD array to vnni_tensor*." Matches the finding's quote. Two overstatements in the finding, neither enough to refute it (see correction): 1. "the design does not say why explicit construction is needed at all" is too strong. The design does give a reason: "The arena currently allocates uninitialized bytes. Accessors cast their addresses to typed block pointers. A newly added owner constructor will not run automatically. Do not cast an existing SIMD-array object to the new owner type." That last sentence paraphrases Ben's requirement without naming him. What the design lacks is the object-lifetime rationale (that today's cast relies on implicit object creation, which the production heap does not formally provide). 2. Recommendation items (2) and (3) largely restate what the design already requires: "Require trivial default construction, copying, and destruction for the new storage", "Keep an explicitly defaulted default constructor ... It must remain trivial", "Do not use value initialization", and "Check the generated code to confirm that construction does not initialize either plane" (design section 6, "Storage and how long its objects remain valid", and section 5 table row "Owner expression constructor / assignment"). Also, kv_block stays an aggregate regardless of vnni_tensor's constructors; the trivial-constructor condition matters for vnni_tensor itself being an implicit-lifetime class, not for kv_block's aggregate status. The design's own hedge ("Verify that the repository's fixed compiler and library versions support the construction method") reduces but does not remove the defect: the sentence still labels the function "supported", and the check has a known answer (not available) that the design should state.
    Correction: The core claim stands as written: std::start_lifetime_as is not available in the pinned toolchain (Clang 19 + libstdc++ from GCC <= 15 via nixpkgs c1cb7d097cb2, where gcc_latest = gcc15; libstdc++ gained it only in GCC 16). Narrow the secondary claim: the design DOES give a reason for explicit construction ("A newly added owner constructor will not run automatically") and DOES paraphrase Ben's no-cast requirement ("Do not cast an existing SIMD-array object to the new owner type"); what it omits is the object-lifetime rationale, namely that the current reinterpret_cast relies on implicit object creation, which aligned_alloc (fake allocator) provides formally and the production custom heap does not. Drop recommendation items (2) and (3) as already present in the design (trivial default construction/copy/destruction required; explicitly defaulted trivial default constructor; no value initialization; generated-code check). Keep only: delete the start_lifetime_as option or mark it "not available in the pinned libstdc++ (GCC 16 only)", and add the one-sentence lifetime rationale with attribution to Ben's comment #3.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's core factual claim holds. The design lists a C++23 lifetime-start function as an option [design-text.txt:426, "A supported C++23 function for starting object lifetimes is another option."]. The only such function is std::start_lifetime_as (paper P2590R2). The repository builds with Clang 19 from nixpkgs [flake.nix:587@f46e48bab498 llvm19 = pkgs.llvmPackages_19;, flake.nix:1107 llvmStdenv = llvm19.stdenv;] and with C++23 [CMakeLists.txt:56 set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 23)]. pkgs is nixpkgs-unstable pinned at c1cb7d097cb2, last modified 2025-12-01 [flake.nix:30,375; flake.lock root inputs]. The Nix CI test build uses that same llvmStdenv [nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:3,13]. On Linux that stdenv uses GNU libstdc++, and the deb runtime depends on libstdc++6 [CMakeLists.txt:837]. cppreference's C++23 library table (fetched 2026-09-22) lists std::start_lifetime_as as first implemented in GCC libstdc++ 16 and not implemented in libc++. GCC 16 did not exist at the 2025-12-01 nixpkgs pin, so the pinned libstdc++ cannot provide the function. The design's option is therefore dead in this toolchain. The design-text fairness check reduces the finding's scope. The design already (a) requires trivial default construction, copying, and destruction of the new storage [design-text.txt:426, :270-272 "It must remain trivial, with no custom initialization", :524 type-trait checks]; (b) asks the implementer to verify toolchain support for the construction method [:434]; (c) asks to check the generated code so construction initializes neither plane [:428]; and (d) states Ben's constraint in paraphrase, "Do not cast an existing SIMD-array object to the new owner type" [:424]. So three of the finding's four recommended additions are already present, and only the "why" and the dead option remain. Residue that stands: the design does not say why explicit construction is needed when the current code never constructs [kv_cache.hpp:1274-1334@f46e48bab498 reinterpret_cast<kv_block_t*>(base); :1202 try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite<uint8_t[]> uninitialized bytes], and it presents an unavailable option without the "not in the pinned libstdc++" caveat. The design's generic "verify support" sentence lowers the cost of that omission, so severity stays minor.
    Correction: Narrowed finding: the design already requires trivial default construction, copy, and destruction for the new owner (lines 270-272, 426, 524), already asks the implementer to verify toolchain support for the construction method (line 434), and already asks to confirm that construction generates no initialization (line 428). Two gaps remain. (1) The "C++23 function for starting object lifetimes" is std::start_lifetime_as; the pinned toolchain (Clang 19 with GNU libstdc++ from nixpkgs-unstable c1cb7d09, 2025-12-01) does not provide it, since libstdc++ first ships it in GCC 16 and libc++ does not ship it at all; delete the option or mark it "not available in the pinned libstdc++". (2) The design does not explain why explicit construction is needed when the current code only reinterpret_casts uninitialized arena bytes to kv_block; add one or two sentences: it is Ben's #3 requirement, and it formally starts the kv_block object lifetimes that the current cast relies on implicitly. The finding's third recommendation (trivial eligible default constructor and trivial destructor on vnni_tensor) is already in the design and needs at most a clause linking it to kv_block staying an implicit-lifetime aggregate.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The factual core holds. (1) Toolchain: flake.nix:1107@f46e48bab498 sets llvmStdenv = llvm19.stdenv; and llvm19 = pkgs.llvmPackages_19 (flake.nix:587) from nixpkgs = haskellNix/nixpkgs-unstable (flake.nix:30), locked at c1cb7d097cb2 (flake.lock, 2025-12-01). At that nixpkgs revision pkgs/development/compilers/gcc/versions.nix lists only GCC 13.4.0, 14.3.0, 15.2.0, and llvm/common/default.nix defines stdenv = overrideCC stdenv buildLlvmPackages.clang where Linux clang resolves to libstdcxxClang (libcxx = null, libstdc++ from GCC). (2) Availability: libstdc++-v3/include/bits/version.def has no start_lifetime_as entry on the gcc-14 and gcc-15 release branches; only GCC master (unreleased in the pinned nixpkgs) adds name = start_lifetime_as; v = 202207; cxxmin = 23. libc++ Cxx23Papers.csv on release/19.x and main lists P2590R2 with empty status. So the design's "supported C++23 function for starting object lifetimes" is not available in the pinned toolchain. (3) Current pattern: kv_cache.hpp:1274-1334@f46e48bab498 does reinterpret_cast<kv_block_t*>(base) on arena bytes with no construction; kv_block (kv_cache.hpp:2190-2248) is a struct with public data members, no user-declared constructors, so it is an aggregate and an implicit-lifetime class. h/system/memory.hpp:323-337 try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite<uint8_t[]> skips the placement-new loop for trivially constructible U, so production bytes come straight from heap->allocate (src/system/memory.cpp:461-469, custom heap, not a [c.malloc] function), while the fake path is aligned_alloc (src/pos/fake.cpp:353-357). The finding's implicit-object-creation reasoning is correct. (4) Intent: Ben #3 says "The page arena must construct the actual storage type"; the finding reinforces that and removes a dead option, so it does not push the design away from Ben's or jhan's intent, and it reduces implementer guesswork. Partial correction: the design already requires trivial default construction, copying and destruction (design-text lines 270, 272, 426) and already asks to check generated code (line 428), so the recommendation's last sentence is redundant; the genuine gaps are the unavailable option and the missing lifetime rationale.
    Correction: The start_lifetime_as option is unavailable in the pinned toolchain (libstdc++ from GCC 13/14/15 in nixpkgs c1cb7d09; libc++ 19 also lacks P2590R2), so delete it or mark it unavailable. The design does not need a new sentence requiring vnni_tensor to keep a trivial default constructor and trivial destructor: it already requires trivial default construction, copying and destruction (design lines 270, 272, 426). The only rationale to add is that explicit placement construction formally starts the kv_block lifetimes that the current reinterpret_cast relies on implicitly, and that default-initialization of a trivially default constructible kv_block emits no instructions (the design already asks to check generated code, line 428).

Minor F41. NaN divergence between set_v truncation and generic conversion unstated

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The core of the finding holds; one worked example is wrong. Verified from primary sources at main f46e48bab498. (1) Design omission: the design text says only "Generic bf16 vector conversion handles NaN differently from the current packed V append instructions." and the test row says "Include defined infinity and NaN cases." (design-text.txt lines 414 and 520). The design nowhere states the rule that generic conversion applies. (2) The float set_v path passes fp32 bits through with no arithmetic: even token _mm512_srli_epi32(_mm512_castps_si512(lo_fp32), 16) [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2380-2382@f46e48bab498]; odd token _mm512_castps_si512(hi_fp32) merged by _mm512_ternarylogic_epi32 with mask 0xffff0000 [kv_cache.hpp:2387-2396]. So fp32 0x7F800001 (a NaN with only the lowest mantissa bit set) becomes bf16 0x7F80 (+Inf). Confirmed. (3) fp32s_to_bf16s (the generic conversion) sets big_mask when xi & 0x7f800000 == 0x7f800000, sets zlsbs_mask when the low 16 bits are zero, and does _mm512_mask_or_epi32(xi, mask, xi, _mm512_set1_epi32(0x00010000)) then _mm512_srli_epi32(y, 16) [h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp:96-108@f46e48bab498]. The AVX2 form does the same with _mm256_or_si256(xi, _mm256_and_si256(mask, _mm256_set1_epi32(0x00010000))) [simd/bf16.hpp:60-71]. So 0x7F800001 becomes 0x7F81 (still a NaN). Confirmed. Inf (low 16 bits zero), finite and denormal inputs (exponent not saturated) skip the OR in both forms and are truncated identically. Confirmed. (4) 8-lane build: save_v_impl calls pg.template v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(slice<...>()(vs[ix].as_view(), ...)) [h/tron/models/model.hpp:2820-2823@f46e48bab498]; the row view is view<bf16, true, false, seq<head_size>> [kv_cache.hpp:1705-1707]; view::set calls chunky<T, aligned>::set(data, i, x.chunk(i)) [h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:189-191]; chunky<bf16, true>::set calls fp32s_to_bf16s(value) [h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:55-58]. So the 8-lane V write does use the generic NaN rule, and the two builds give different bits for such inputs. Confirmed. (5) The fp16 worked example is WRONG. VCVTPH2PS quiets a signalling NaN (x86 default for a masked invalid-operation exception). Measured on this host (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, F16C and AVX-512F present) with hand-assembled VEX.128 and EVEX.512 VCVTPH2PS stubs executed from anonymous memory, no file written: fp16 0x7C01 -> fp32 0x7FC02000 (not 0x7F802000 as the finding says); fp16 0x7C07 -> 0x7FC0E000; fp16 0x7C00 -> 0x7F800000 and 0x3C00 -> 0x3F800000 as sanity checks. So set_v(fp16*) stores bf16 0x7FC0 (a quiet NaN), not +Inf 0x7F80, and the generic path stores 0x7FC1. The two paths still produce different bits for fp16 NaN inputs, but the NaN-becomes-Inf hazard exists only for the float source path. Caveat: the Intel production target (delphi-3bda) was not measured here; the quieting behavior is the architectural x86 rule shared by Intel and AMD. Severity: minor stands. The design's instruction to keep the existing instructions and not substitute generic conversion (design-text.txt line 406) already prevents an implementer from changing behavior; the omission only affects how the test's independently chosen expected bits are derived.
    Correction: Keep the finding but replace the fp16 example. Corrected divergence rule: set_v(float*) keeps the upper 16 bits of the fp32 input unchanged, so an fp32 NaN whose upper 7 mantissa bits are zero becomes +/-Inf (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F80). fp32s_to_bf16s first ORs 0x00010000 into any input with a saturated exponent and nonzero low 16 bits, so the result stays a NaN (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F81). For the fp16 path, _mm512_cvtph_ps quiets a signalling NaN before the truncation: fp16 0x7C01 -> fp32 0x7FC02000, so set_v stores 0x7FC0 (a quiet NaN, not +Inf) and the generic path stores 0x7FC1. The two paths therefore differ in bits for fp16 NaN inputs too, but the NaN-becomes-Inf hazard exists only for the float source path. Infinity, denormal and finite inputs convert identically in both paths. Recommendation unchanged otherwise: state the rule in the design and choose per-build expected bits (16-lane packed append = pure truncation of the fp32 bits after any fp16 conversion; 8-lane row-major append = fp32s_to_bf16s).
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The omission is real but smaller than F41 says, and F41's own statement of the rule is wrong in two places. What the design already covers: line 406 of the design text states the packed-V rule exactly ("the existing conversion to fp32 followed by keeping its upper 16 bits"); line 408 says the AVX2 row-major V append keeps the existing row-view .set() conversion and its NaN/infinity handling; line 414 says the generic conversion differs and cites [S7], whose link is h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp#L92-L109 at f46e48bab498. So the design gives the packed rule fully and the generic rule by reference. What the design gets wrong, and F41 repeats: the generic rule depends on a build macro. bf16.hpp:93 (16-lane) and bf16.hpp:57 (8-lane) at f46e48bab498 read #ifdef TRON_IGNORE_NAN, and in that branch fp32s_to_bf16s (the generic fp32-to-bf16 conversion) is pure truncation with no NaN quieting. src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:267-270 at f46e48bab498 sets target_compile_options(tron PUBLIC "$<$<AND:${CXX_GccOrClang},$<NOT:$<CONFIG:Debug>>>:-O3;-DTRON_IGNORE_NAN;-DTRON_IGNORE_FTZ>"), and src/rinzler/CMakeLists.txt:61 does the same. The Nix CI test build uses -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo (nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:80 at f46e48bab498), and the native/deb presets are RelWithDebInfo (CMakePresets.json:37,74); only asan/tsan/coverage presets are Debug (lines 89,102,114). So in the CI test build and in production, fp32s_to_bf16s and set_v give identical bits for every input, including NaN. The design sentence "Generic bf16 vector conversion handles NaN differently" holds only in Debug builds, and F41's "the result is always a NaN" and "the two builds legitimately give different bits" hold only in Debug builds. Second error: F41's fp16 example. I ran _mm256_cvtph_ps on this host (F16C present) with fp16 0x7C01: the result is fp32 0x7FC02000, not 0x7F802000, because the hardware conversion sets the quiet bit on a signaling NaN (Intel SDM Vol. 1 Table 4-7, single-operand SNaN rule). set_v's srli 16 then gives 0x7FC0 (a bf16 NaN), not 0x7F80 (+Inf). The fp16 path therefore never turns a NaN into infinity; the only possible difference is the payload bit (0x7FC0 vs 0x7FC1), and only in Debug builds. F41's other evidence holds: kv_cache.hpp:2342 and 2382 srli 16 even paths, 2354-2358 and 2393-2397 ternarylogic odd paths; chunky.hpp:50-57 chunky<bf16,true>::set calls fp32s_to_bf16s; view.hpp:187-191 view::set calls chunky::set. The finding is not refuted because the design does not state the generic rule or its build dependence, and its "handles NaN differently" sentence is unconditional when it should be conditional. It is corrected because F41's proposed replacement text would put wrong expected bits into the design.
    Correction: Corrected claim: the design states the packed-V rule exactly (fp32 bits, keep the upper 16) and points to the generic rule by link, but its sentence "Generic bf16 vector conversion handles NaN differently from the current packed V append instructions" is unconditional and is only true in Debug builds. fp32s_to_bf16s (h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp:56-71 and 92-109 at f46e48bab498) quiets NaN only when TRON_IGNORE_NAN is undefined; src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:267-270 defines TRON_IGNORE_NAN for every non-Debug build, and the CI Nix test build is RelWithDebInfo (nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:80). In CI and production both paths truncate identically for all inputs. Suggested replacement sentence: "In Debug builds the generic conversion sets bit 16 of an fp32 NaN whose low 16 bits are nonzero before truncating (0x7F800001 becomes 0x7F81); in RelWithDebInfo and Release builds TRON_IGNORE_NAN is defined and it truncates like set_v (0x7F800001 becomes 0x7F80, which is +Inf). The packed V append always truncates." The test row should say that NaN expected bits for the generic (8-lane row-view .set()) path depend on TRON_IGNORE_NAN, so a hard-coded NaN expectation would pass in one build type and fail in the other. Drop F41's fp16 example: _mm256_cvtph_ps quiets a signaling NaN (fp16 0x7C01 -> fp32 0x7FC02000 -> bf16 0x7FC0 in both paths in non-Debug builds; 0x7FC1 only in the Debug generic path); the fp16 path never yields infinity from a NaN. Only the fp32 source path with a signaling NaN (quiet bit clear, e.g. 0x7F800001) can collapse to +Inf, and it does so in both paths under TRON_IGNORE_NAN.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The core claim holds; I checked every cited location at f46e48bab498. (1) The packed set_v float path truncates fp32 bits: bf16x32 even = _mm512_srli_epi32(_mm512_castps_si512(lo_fp32), 16); [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2382@f46e48bab498; fp16 path at 2342 after _mm512_cvtph_ps at 2340; odd paths merge via _mm512_ternarylogic_epi32 at 2355 and 2394]. No NaN handling exists there. (2) The generic conversion forces a NaN bit before truncating: uint32x16 y = _mm512_mask_or_epi32(xi, mask, xi, _mm512_set1_epi32(0x00010000)); then y = _mm512_srli_epi32(y, 16); where mask = exponent saturated AND low 16 bits nonzero [h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp:92-109, lines 106 and 108]; the AVX2 form at bf16.hpp:56-71 (line 71 _mm256_or_si256(xi, _mm256_and_si256(mask, _mm256_set1_epi32(0x00010000)))) applies the same rule. So fp32 0x7F800001 gives 0x7F80 (+Inf) through set_v and 0x7F81 (NaN) through fp32s_to_bf16s, as the finding says. (3) The 8-lane build really does go through the generic path: model.hpp:2820-2823@f46e48bab498 #else pg.template v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(slice<...>()(vs[ix].as_view(), ...)); view::set calls chunky<T, aligned>::set(data, i, x.chunk(i)) [h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:191]; chunky<bf16, true>::set calls fp32s_to_bf16s(value) [h/tron/kernels/chunky.hpp:50-57]. (4) The design sentences quoted are verbatim (design-text.txt lines 414 and 520). The design states the mechanism ("The existing conversion to fp32 followed by keeping its upper 16 bits") but never states the observable consequence, while its test row asks for independently chosen NaN/Inf expected bits. INTENT lens: neither Ben (#2/#3) nor jhan's prompt mentions conversion numerics, but the issue body and the design itself make "preserve conversions" a requirement, so spelling out the rule serves that stated intent rather than pushing away from it. It is not pure taste: an implementer who sees NaN become +Inf in the packed path and does not know that is the preserved behavior could "fix" it and change stored bits. Consequence is limited because the mechanism is already stated, so minor is the right severity. One factual correction to the finding's fp16 example (see correction).
    Correction: The fp32 worked example (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F80 via set_v, 0x7F81 via fp32s_to_bf16s) is correct. The fp16 worked example is wrong in its outcome. set_v uses the hardware instruction VCVTPH2PS (_mm512_cvtph_ps, kv_cache.hpp:2340@f46e48bab498). Per the Intel SDM (Vol 1 section 4.8.3.4, Table 4-7; VCVTPH2PS lists the Invalid exception), a signaling NaN source is returned as its quiet form when the exception is masked, so fp16 0x7C01 becomes fp32 0x7FC02000, not 0x7F802000. Every fp16 NaN therefore reaches fp32 with bit 22 set, and set_v truncation yields 0x7FC0 (a NaN), never +Inf. The fp16 divergence is only the low bit: set_v gives 0x7FC0, the generic conversion gives 0x7FC1 (low 16 bits 0x2000 are nonzero, so 0x00010000 is ORed in). The "NaN becomes +/-Inf" property exists only for the float source path. I did not execute the instruction to confirm the quieting (no files may be written); the claim rests on the SDM rule. The recommendation should read: "For float sources, pure truncation can turn a NaN into +/-Inf (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F80) while fp32s_to_bf16s gives 0x7F81. For fp16 sources, both results are NaN but differ in the low bit (0x7C01 -> 0x7FC0 vs 0x7FC1)." The per-build statement about expected values (8-lane follows fp32s_to_bf16s, 16-lane follows truncation) stands.

Minor F42. Typed scaled_v_expr: keep count argument and pair round-up

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Evidence checked at main f46e48bab498, h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp: members const bf16s* const data; (line 1992) and const size_t count; (1993); constructor at 2066-2078 takes const size_t count and computes const size_t end = (count + 1) / 2 * 2; (2078); 8-lane loop for (size_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) (2157); page::scaled_v passes count() (2261). All four citations hold. The failure scenario also holds: the caller sets every one of the 64 score lanes to -inf and then exponentiates, so weights at rows >= count are 0 [self_attention.hpp:1565-1566 and 1588 at f46e48ba], and rows beyond count are uninitialized arena bytes (the design says "Do not clear the arena"), so a 64-row loop would feed possible NaN rows times 0 into _mm512_dpbf16_ps, which gives NaN. However, the finding overstates the omission. The design already fixes the loop bound in prose: "When the number of used token positions in the page is odd, the weighted V sum also reads the following unused row" (design-text.txt line 375) is exactly the (count+1)/2*2 bound and is false for a 64-row loop. It also says the tensor does not carry the count: "Keep selection of valid tokens there [cache and model code] too. ... The tensor represents values and shape. It does not track whether a token is ready" (line 396), and "Keep the current unrolled loops" (line 412). What the design does not say is only that step 4's re-typed scaled_v_expr constructor keeps the separate count argument fed from page::count(); step 4 (line 460) names only the V parameter change. So the finding stands as a narrower omission.
    Correction: Omission, narrower than stated: the design already fixes the weighted-sum loop bound at the used-token count rounded up by one row when odd (section 6, line 375) and already says the tensor type does not track valid tokens (section 6, line 396), so a 64-row loop would contradict the design as written. What step 4 does not say is that the scaled_v_expr constructor keeps its separate count argument (from page::count()) next to the new typed V view; add that one sentence to step 4. The 8-lane bound (count rows) and 16-lane bound ((count+1)/2*2 rows) can be restated there for the implementer's convenience, but they are not absent from the design.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Source evidence in the finding holds at f46e48bab498. kv_cache.hpp:1991-1993 declares const bf16s* const data; and const size_t count;. The 16-lane constructor (kv_cache.hpp:2066-2078) takes count and computes const size_t end = (count + 1) / 2 * 2;, and all its loops run i < end. The 8-lane loop (kv_cache.hpp:2157) runs i < count. page::scaled_v (kv_cache.hpp:2260-2261) passes count() as the third constructor argument. The failure scenario is accurate: self_attention.hpp:1549-1550 initialises s_pages to -inf, so exp(s - m) is exactly 0 for every row beyond count, and a loop over the view's 64 static rows would multiply stale rows by 0, where a stale NaN poisons the sum (Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots], kv_cache.hpp:2054-2065). Design-text fairness check: the design covers the point only partly, so the finding is not refuted. Three sentences point the implementer the right way without stating the rule: Section 6 line 375 "When the number of used token positions in the page is odd, the weighted V sum also reads the following unused row" (describes the pair round-up); line 396 "Keep selection of valid tokens there [cache and model code] too ... The tensor represents values and shape. It does not track whether a token is ready" (implies the view carries no count); table line 412 "Keep the current unrolled loops ... Keep the supported shapes" (implies keeping their bounds). No sentence says count stays a constructor argument sourced from page::count(), and no sentence says the view's static row count is not a loop bound. The step-4 wording "a typed V input" names only the data argument. The design's safety net does not close the gap either. Section 8 line 518 relies on retaining the existing test "scaled_v padding NaN" (t_llama_unit.cpp:2328-2515 at f46e48bab498). That test poisons only the padding partner: token 7 with count 7 (SECTION "append_v preserves poison") and slot 1 with count 1 (SECTION "single-token dirty-buffer reuse"). Rows 8..63 are left as arena bytes from try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite (uninitialized, kv_cache.hpp:1187-1206). A 64-row loop therefore passes that test whenever those bytes happen not to be NaN, so the omission is not caught by the verification the design prescribes.
    Correction: Narrow the claim: the design already says the weighted V sum reads the used token positions plus one following unused row when the used count is odd [design line 375], that valid-token selection stays in cache and model code and the tensor does not track readiness [line 396], and that the current unrolled loops must be kept [line 412]. The remaining omission is that no sentence states that count stays a separate scaled_v_expr constructor argument sourced from page::count(), nor that the typed view's static row count (64 tokens) is never a loop bound. Add to the recommendation: the retained test "scaled_v padding NaN" poisons only the padding partner (token 7 with count 7; slot 1 with count 1), so it does not detect a loop over all 64 rows; the design should either name this explicit rule for the implementer or ask for the test to poison rows beyond count+1 as well.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The cited source facts hold at main f46e48bab498. scaled_v_expr (the weighted V sum) stores const bf16s* const data; and const size_t count; [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1992-1993]. The 16-lane constructor takes count and sets end = (count + 1) / 2 * 2 [kv_cache.hpp:2078]. The 8-lane constructor loops for (size_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) [kv_cache.hpp:2157]. page::scaled_v passes count() (the page's valid-token count) as the third argument [kv_cache.hpp:2261]. The failure scenario is real, not a taste matter. The attention caller initializes all 64 score lanes to -inf before the QK loop [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1549], so exp(s - m) is exactly 0 for every lane at or beyond count. The comment at self_attention.hpp:1511-1513 says page.count() skips "stale entries from other branches", so rows beyond count hold old or uninitialized bytes (the arena is allocated uninitialized, facts.md line 55). A loop over the view's static 64 rows would therefore multiply stale bit patterns by 0, and NaN * 0 = NaN, exactly as the finding says. The design does omit the explicit statement. Step 4 says only "Give scaled_v_expr, the existing weighted V sum, a typed V input. Obtain its physical address only inside that implementation. Preserve instruction order." [design-text.txt line 460; HTML line 328]. Section 6's "Weighted V sum" row says "Keep the current unrolled loops... Keep the supported shapes." with no loop bound [design-text.txt line 412]. Neither states that count remains a constructor argument or that the view's 64-row shape is not the bound. Replacing const bf16s* data with a view that carries Rows=64 invites an implementer to treat count as redundant. The finding does not conflict with Ben's or jhan's intent. Ben's straw-man covers only the AMX kernel signatures (qk_vnni_128x4, weights_times_v_128x4 taking packed_k_page / packed_v_page) and never mentions scaled_v_expr or a count parameter [evidence/github-review.json, comment #3]. jhan's prompt (codex-add-tensor-type.md) does not touch the weighted sum. Keeping count outside the view matches the design's own rule "The tensor represents values and shape. It does not track whether a token is ready." [design-text.txt line 396]. The finding is overstated in one place, corrected below: the pair round-up IS stated in prose in Section 6, "When the number of used token positions in the page is odd, the weighted V sum also reads the following unused row." [design-text.txt line 375; HTML line 273], and the zero-partner rule is already stated there too. So the recommendation's last sentence ("The padding row read by the round-up is zero only because the append path zeroed it") repeats existing text. Severity stays minor: the surrounding text ("Preserve instruction order", "existing order of additions", "Keep the current unrolled loops", "Keep selection of valid tokens [in the cache]") constrains an attentive implementer, but one explicit sentence removes a guess on a hot path with a NaN hazard.
    Correction: Omission, narrowed: the design already states the pair round-up in prose (Section 6: the sum "also reads the following unused row" when the used-token count is odd) and already states that valid-token selection stays in the cache, not the tensor. What it does not state is (a) that scaled_v_expr keeps count (passed as page::count()) as a separate constructor argument alongside the typed V view and scratch, and (b) that the view's static 64-row shape is never the loop bound: the 16-lane bound stays (count + 1) / 2 * 2 rows and the 8-lane bound stays count rows. Add those two sentences to step 4 or the "Weighted V sum" row. Drop the recommendation's last sentence about the padding row being zero because of the append path; Section 6 already says it.

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's evidence holds. I checked each cited source myself. 1. The design quote is verbatim. That covers ownership, alignment and size for V only. It does not cover the child's K rule, which is what the step-7 sentence refers to. So the omission in step 7 stands, but "does not say where it is written" is slightly overstated. 5. It comes from the caller: src/tron/gof.cpp at 30c4ac82cbb6 lines 203-219. populate() declares alignas(64) std::array<bf16, HW_MAX_KV_HEADS * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE> k_scratch as a local (line 203), passes a distinct slice per kv head (k_scratch.data() + h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE, line 213), collects the returned pointers in k_heads[], and consumes them all in k.set_position() (line 218) before the next token iteration reuses the same slices.     Correction: Omission: the step-7 sentence "Preserve the child PR's existing rules for how long that buffer remains valid" names neither the rule nor its child-source location. The design does cite the main-side V contract (gof.hpp#L58-L66 at f46e48ba via S9), but not the child K rule. The rule is split across two places at 30c4ac82cb: gof.hpp:63-73 (caller-owned, 64-byte-aligned scratch of HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE bf16 per row; the callback returns the scratch when the layout spreads a token) and src/tron/gof.cpp:203-219 (populate() owns a local alignas(64) array, hands each kv head its own slice, and consumes every returned pointer in set_position() before the next token reuses the slices). Recommended replacement: "The caller (gof::populate) owns a 64-byte-aligned scratch with one HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE bf16 slice per kv head. The returned pointer is valid only until populate() reuses that slice for the next token [gof.hpp:63-73 and src/tron/gof.cpp:203-219 at 30c4ac82cb]."
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding's evidence holds and the design only partly covers the point. (1) h/tron/gof.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 58-79 contain the quoted comment verbatim: "k_head_fn(slot, kv_head, scratch) -> bf16* ... the callback rebuilds the row in the caller-owned, 64-byte-aligned scratch and returns the scratch". (2) h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 2644-2661 implement k_head_fn with the dest scratch parameter (get_k_row into dest under k_vnni::layout_on, else return the page pointer). (3) The design HTML links only the MAIN versions: f46e48ba gof.hpp#L58-L66 and full.hpp#L2644-L2665 (S9). It has no link to the child gof.hpp, child full.hpp, or gof.cpp. The criticized Section 7 sentence ("Preserve the child PR's existing rules for how long that buffer remains valid") carries no citation. (4) Design-text fairness check: Section 6 does say "Unpack packed V into the existing temporary buffer supplied by the caller ... PR #4424 adds a parameter for a temporary buffer needed by packed K. Keep that change in the child PR. [S9]", and main gof.hpp@f46e48ba lines 63-64 (linked via S9) state the V form of the rule ("caller-owned, 64-byte-aligned scratch, which holds HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE bf16 values"). So caller ownership is conveyed and the V rule is reachable. But the design never states the 64-byte alignment or the HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE size, never cites where the K rule is written in the child, and points at a "how long it remains valid" rule that the child gof.hpp comment does not actually state. (5) The finding itself is slightly off on where the lifetime rule lives: gof.hpp states ownership/alignment/return convention only; the lifetime comes from the caller src/tron/gof.cpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 203-219 (alignas(64) std::array k_scratch/v_scratch local to populate(); per-head slices k_scratch.data() + h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE; pointers consumed by set_position at line 218 before the next pos iteration reuses the scratch). The finding's recommended sentence should cite gof.cpp too. Severity minor is right: this is in the child-update section, the parent PR keeps the K callback unchanged, and a child implementer would meet the gof.hpp comment while rebasing, but the design sends the implementer to preserve rules it never names or pins, which is an actionability gap.
    Correction: Omission, partly covered: the design does say the buffer is "supplied by the caller" (Section 6, [S9] -> main gof.hpp#L58-L66, which states the V form of the rule), so caller ownership is reachable. What is missing is the concrete rule for the K callback in the child and its location. Replace the Section 7 sentence with: "The caller, gof::populate, owns a 64-byte-aligned scratch of HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE bf16 values per head, local to one populate() call. The callback returns either a pointer into the page or that scratch. The returned pointer is consumed by set_position for that token position before the next position reuses the scratch [gof.hpp:58-79, src/tron/gof.cpp:203-219, scheduler/full.hpp:2644-2661 at 30c4ac82cb]." Note that gof.hpp states ownership, alignment, and the return convention only; the lifetime is defined by the caller in gof.cpp.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The finding holds, with one refinement of where the rule actually lives. Checks made: (1) The design sentence is verbatim at design-new-tensor-type.html line 350 ("Update the K callback used for hardware preparation. It must unpack a logical row into the caller's temporary buffer. Preserve the child PR's existing rules for how long that buffer remains valid.") and carries no citation. (2) The only gof.hpp link in the whole page is at html line 452, under S9, and it points to MAIN: github.com/positron-ai/tron/blob/f46e48bab498.../h/tron/gof.hpp#L58-L66. At f46e48ba, gof.hpp:61 and :65 declare k_head_fn WITHOUT a scratch parameter ("k_head_fn(slot, kv_head) -> bf16* to K data for this token."). So an implementer following the design's own link sees a K callback that has no scratch, which contradicts the sentence they are asked to act on. The design never links the child's gof.hpp. (3) The child comment exists as the finding says: gof.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 61-77 ("When the stored layout spreads the token's values, the callback rebuilds the row in the caller-owned, 64-byte-aligned scratch and returns the scratch", with the K-under-TRON_K_VNNI bullet); confirmed via git diff f46e48bab498 30c4ac82cbb6 -- h/tron/gof.hpp. (4) full.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6: the k_head_fn lambda with bf16* dest runs lines 2644-2661 (get_k_row into dest and return dest at 2656-2657; the finding's 2644-2653 range is slightly short but points at the right code). (5) Intent lens: Ben's comments #2/#3 do not address the GOF callback, and jhan asked for a design "another agent will implement"; adding a citation moves toward that intent, not away from it. Not taste: the design's only link for this callback shows the pre-child signature. Refinement: the child gof.hpp comment states ownership and alignment, not the duration of validity. The duration comes from the caller gof::populate at src/tron/gof.cpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 203-217: k_scratch is a 64-byte-aligned stack array local to populate(), sliced per head (h * HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE), reused for each of the 4 GOF token positions, and consumed by k.set_position(pos, k_heads, ...) in the same loop iteration (diff vs main adds k_scratch at 203 and the third argument at 213). Severity: minor. The implementer rebases the child and would see k_scratch during conflict resolution, so they would not build the wrong thing, but the text should change because its only link shows the wrong signature.
    Correction: Omission (as filed) with a corrected source: the child's gof.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 61-77 state WHO owns the scratch (caller-owned, 64-byte aligned) and that the callback returns it, but not HOW LONG the returned pointer stays valid. That duration is set by the caller gof::populate in src/tron/gof.cpp@30c4ac82cbb6 lines 203-217 (k_scratch is a stack array inside populate(), one HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE slice per head, reused for each of the 4 GOF token positions, consumed by k.set_position() in the same loop iteration). The recommended replacement sentence should read: "The caller (gof::populate) owns the 64-byte-aligned scratch, one HW_BAKED_HEAD_SIZE slice per head. The returned pointer is valid only until populate() reuses that slice for the next token position [gof.hpp:61-77, src/tron/gof.cpp:203-217, full.hpp:2644-2661 at 30c4ac82cb]." Also fix the S9 "Callback source" link: it points to main gof.hpp#L58-L66 where k_head_fn has no scratch parameter, so it does not show the rule the sentence refers to.

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Checked the design HTML, the design text, and kv_cache.hpp at main f46e48bab498. Section 5 (HTML lines 239, 264) says only "Place append_v_row in the internal tensor operation implementation" and "Keep direct storage access in the internal helper detail::vnni_access" — a namespace, still no file. (3) Include direction: nothing in the design states that kv_cache.hpp includes vnni.hpp; it is only implied by step 3 (kv_block must hold the typed owner). The design never says which two headers could form a cycle. The code to move uses only immintrin intrinsics, the aliases bf16x16/bf16x32/fp16x16/fp32x16 (h/tron/simd/types.hpp:26-31 at f46e48ba) and the scalar bf16/fp16 types (set_v/get_v bodies kv_cache.hpp:2281-2473; append_v kv_cache.hpp:1866-1924). Minor F47. 8-lane and other build configurations lack flags and CI lane

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Every cited source line holds at f46e48bab498. h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:17-21 reads #if defined(TRON_AVX512_ENABLED) / #define TRON_CHUNK_SIZE 16 / #else / #define TRON_CHUNK_SIZE 8. src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:244-246 reads if(AVX512) ... target_compile_definitions(tron PUBLIC TRON_AVX512_ENABLED). CMakeLists.txt:44 reads option(AVX512 "Enable AVX512" ON), and the BUILD_NATIVE probe at CMakeLists.txt:95-117 only executes set(AVX512 OFF) when a required feature is missing from lscpu output. nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:86-88 passes -DBUILD_NATIVE=OFF and -DAVX512=ON (flake.nix:1206-1208 does the same for the sandbox-configure check). .github/workflows/cmake-single-platform.yml:151 has runs-on: [self-hosted, fpga, test, avx512] and :353-355 runs cmake ... --preset native ... -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON. CMakePresets.json:49-66 defines preset "darwin" with "AVX512": "OFF", the only preset that does so; GNUmakefile:216-218 selects it only when uname -s is Darwin. git grep -i "avx512\|darwin\|preset" f46e48bab498 -- .github nix returns only avx512 runner labels, the preset-native line, and the Nix -DAVX512=ON; no workflow sets AVX512=OFF or uses the darwin preset. h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:30-35 has #error "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 ...", so the 8-lane configuration cannot be combined with AMX. On the design side, plain grep of status/design-new-tensor-type.html finds 0 occurrences of "AVX512", "TRON_CHUNK_SIZE" and "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH"; the only 8-lane mentions are the storage sentence (HTML line 301) and the quoted acceptance row (line 370), and the "Missing parent CI evidence" paragraph (line 383) speaks only about the Nix build leaving AMX disabled. One nuance that does not refute the finding: cpp-coverage.yml (weekly schedule + manual dispatch, runs-on [self-hosted, fpga, test] without the avx512 label) runs cmake --preset coverage (GNUmakefile:609), which leaves BUILD_NATIVE ON and lets the host probe decide, so it could produce an 8-lane build by accident on a runner without AVX-512; it never selects that configuration deliberately and is not among the required checks listed in README.ci.md:74-77. Whether any such runner exists is Insufficient data (would need the runner inventory).

GNUmakefile:217-218 selects it only on macOS. git grep 'AVX512\|darwin' f46e48bab498 -- .github returns nothing, so no workflow builds with AVX512 off. - h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:30-35: #error "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16", confirming the 8-lane configuration cannot be combined with AMX (R62's point). - The TRON_CHUNK_UNSUPPORTED static_asserts (expr.hpp, int4.hpp, permuted.hpp, quantization.hpp, safetensors.hpp) all sit in #else branches after #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 / #elif == 16, so they do not prove the 8-lane build is broken; whether unmodified main compiles at AVX512=OFF is Insufficient data without a build, which is exactly what the finding says the design leaves open. Design-text fairness check (whole design-text.txt read): - The strings AVX512, TRON_AVX512_ENABLED, TRON_CHUNK_SIZE, TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, darwin, and preset appear nowhere in the design text or the HTML markup (grep on design-new-tensor-type.html returned nothing). So the last sentence of the finding's recommendation is redundant with the design. The rest of the finding stands.
    Correction: The finding holds except for one redundant sentence in its recommendation. Keep: the design names the 8-lane row-major V configuration as a required build-and-test target (line 526) without naming the option that selects it (AVX512=OFF, which makes TRON_CHUNK_SIZE 8 via TRON_AVX512_ENABLED), without saying that no CI job at f46e48bab498 builds it (Nix and the manual CMake lane both force AVX512 ON; only the macOS-only darwin preset turns it off), and without saying whether unmodified main compiles in it.   • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). I checked every cited source at f46e48bab498 and all of it holds. h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:17-21 defines TRON_CHUNK_SIZE 16 under TRON_AVX512_ENABLED and 8 otherwise. src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:244-247 defines TRON_AVX512_ENABLED only under if(AVX512). Intent lens: Ben's comments #2/#3 say nothing about build configurations, and jhan's prompt says "Another agent will implement the design by reading this file", so adding the build flags and the CI-coverage gap serves the handoff without moving the design away from either intent. It is not a matter of taste: the design's own definition of done says "Name every hardware or build-configuration check that was not run", and an implementer cannot do that for the 8-lane build without knowing how it is selected and that no CI job exercises it. The recommended command (cmake --preset native -DAVX512=OFF) is valid because the probe can only turn AVX512 off, never back on. Consequence is limited to an implementer having to look these facts up or skipping the 8-lane build unnoticed, so minor is the right severity.

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The omission is real. It delegates by name to the run-tron-tests skill (design lines 534, 540, 544) and links that skill, AGENTS.md and t/AGENTS.md under S12 (design-new-tensor-type.html lines 469-471). README.ci.md:657-660 also names the local reproduction as make build-test, make format, make lint. The design's phrase "required static checks" is ambiguous because the repo has two different sets (make lint is a required CI check per README.ci.md:93; make iwyu-check applies only to a "public API header" per SKILL.md:65), and the design does not say which applies. Two sub-claims in the recommendation are wrong or incomplete, corrected below.
    Correction: Keep the finding, but fix the recommendation. (1) The parent's headers are not "public API headers" under the repo's own definition: AGENTS.md@98bb8cb22f94 lines 71-72 say "The public C++ API is h/libtron.hpp plus the headers it IWYU-exports", and h/libtron.hpp@98bb8cb22f94 lines 14-17 export only tron/constrained/constraint.hpp, tron/models/config.hpp, tron/models/logits.hpp and tron/scheduler/token_cache_control.hpp. kv_cache.hpp, amx_attn_iface.hpp and tensor/slice.hpp are not among them, so the SKILL.md:65-66 iwyu-check branch is not triggered by definition; the SKILL.md:55-56 branch (t_<name>, make build-test, make test-host) is the required one, and make iwyu-check is an optional extra (bin/ci/run-iwyu.sh analyzes src/ and t/, with h/ headers reached only through includers). (2) The command list should include make lint (GNUmakefile:693; a required CI check name per README.ci.md:93; README.ci.md:659-660 lists make format then make lint). (3) "bin/slice bench --update t_llama_unit" does need a whole-box grant (bin/slice@98bb8cb22f94:2002 _require_whole_box_dice_grant(total, "bench")), but config/test-benchmarks.json is platform-keyed (top-level blocks genoa96, genoa32, granite_rapids_6960p, granite_rapids_6962p) and bench writes only the detected platform's block (bin/slice:1731-1736 put_platform_bench, get_server_type from resource-map.yaml). A run on delphi-3bda (a 6962P) refreshes only the granite_rapids_6962p block; the other three blocks need their own hosts or must be named as not refreshed. Corrected list to name in the design: make format; make lint; build and run t_llama_unit, t_amx_dispatch_dtype, t_amx_numerics; make build-test; make test-host; optionally make iwyu-check; bin/slice bench --update t_llama_unit per platform block, with the 3bda lease rule.
