Note C09. Construction pass needs the slot-spec loop; allocate_kv_group has only a byte count

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). I re-derived every cited line at f46e48bab498 and the finding holds. allocate_kv_group takes only (bool reclaimable, size_t bytes) and returns true at once when bytes is 0 [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1199-1208@f46e48bab498]. storage_location returns {reclaimable, byte_offset} with a per-page byte offset [:726-735]. slot_base multiplies that offset by n_pages and adds it to the chosen arena [:1187-1197]. kv_block_at_storage casts slot_base(offset) to kv_block<page_size, geometry.head_size>* and indexes blocks[kv_head * n_pages + pg] with kv_head below geometry.n_kv_heads [:1301-1334]. The uniform-reclaimability path indexes blocks[(slot.i * n_kv_heads + kv_head) * n_pages + pg] over kv_blocks_alias [:1276-1290]. Heterogeneous layouts really do give each slot its own geometry and its own arena offset: kv_slot_spec carries a kv_geometry {n_kv_heads, head_size} per slot [:104-109, :154-159], and make_kv_slot_offsets accumulates slot_bytes = n_kv_heads * 2 * 64 * sizeof(bf16) * head_size per slot into per-arena prefix offsets [:646-672]. So one typed array per slot, placed at arena + n_pages * byte_offset, is the only shape that matches the accessors in the non-uniform case, and one array over the arena works only when uniform_geometry holds (EAGLE storage requires uniform geometry, eagle_compatible at :771 and the assert at :1328). The restore path calls allocate_kv_group(true, reclaimable_kv_bytes_) and then refresh_kv_blocks_alias [:962-973], so the construction pass must also filter slots by arena, as the recommendation says. The design text names none of these hooks: a grep of design-new-tensor-type.html for allocate_kv_group, storage_location, slot_base, kv_block_at_storage, refresh_kv_blocks_alias, slot_specs and storage_slot_count returns zero hits; the design only links S8 to the regions kv_cache.hpp#L1187-, #L1274- and #L946-. Its quoted sentences ("Cover every slot geometry and the extra EAGLE region. Do this once per allocation.") state the requirement without a mechanism. Two small corrections to the finding are below. I could not check the panel's F12 text (not supplied), so the sentence about F12 is unverified but not load-bearing.
    Correction: Three refinements, none of which change the verdict. (1) The per-slot array length is spec.geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages, not the book's template n_kv_heads * n_pages: n_kv_heads is part of each slot's kv_geometry [kv_cache.hpp:104-109@f46e48bab498] and make_kv_slot_offsets sizes each slot with specs[slot].geometry.n_kv_heads [:646-672]. (2) allocate_kv_group is a book member function, so it can see layout_t, logical_slot_count(), n_pages and both arenas; "receives only (reclaimable, bytes)" is true of its parameters only, and the slot loop can live inside it, filtered by storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable == reclaimable. (3) The codebase already has the loop the finding asks for: for_each_active_slot iterates the active slots with slot index and kv_geometry as template arguments [:1229-1243], and fill_random uses it plus a separate call at eagle_storage_offset() with primary_geometry for the EAGLE region [:1055-1065, :906-909]. The recommendation should name for_each_active_slot and eagle_storage_offset as the precedent instead of describing a new loop. Also, "gives the agent none of these hooks" should read "links the allocation and block-access regions (S8) but names no hook and no loop".
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Evidence check (all at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498, read with git show): allocate_kv_group(bool reclaimable, size_t bytes) at 1199-1208 starts with if (bytes == 0) return true; and only allocates and emplaces an arena; storage_location at 727-735 returns {reclaimable, byte_offset}; slot_base at 1188-1197 returns arena->get() + n_pages * location.byte_offset; the uniform kv_block accessor at 1276-1290 indexes blocks[(slot.i * n_kv_heads + kv_head) * n_pages + pg] over kv_blocks_alias; kv_block_at_storage at 1301-1334 indexes blocks[kv_head * n_pages + pg] after reinterpret_cast<kv_block_t*>(slot_base(offset)) and asserts kv_head < geometry.n_kv_heads. Both callers of allocate_kv_group (constructor 1101-1102, restore_reclaimable_kv_storage 968) are the two moments the design names. So the finding's facts hold. Fairness check against the whole design text: the design states the requirement ("Create the typed block arrays in each new arena before other code can access it ... Create these typed arrays on initial allocation and when restoring a released arena. Cover every slot geometry and the extra EAGLE region. Do this once per allocation." and "It must work for slots with different geometries.") and its S8 link "[Allocation source]" points to kv_cache.hpp#L1187-L1206, which contains slot_base and allocate_kv_group, while "[Block access source]" points to #L1274-L1334, which contains both index formulas. So the design does hand the agent the hooks by link. It does not name them in prose, does not say the block type varies per slot geometry in a heterogeneous layout (fixed_kv_layout), and does not point to the existing per-slot iteration idiom (for_each_active_slot at 1242-1250 plus the EAGLE branch, exactly as fill_random does at 1057-1065). That is partial coverage, so the finding is not refuted, but its sentence "gives the agent none of these hooks" is overstated. Two small corrections to the finding's own recommendation: (1) the per-slot array length is spec.geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages, not the book's template n_kv_heads (make_kv_slot_offsets at 651-672 multiplies specs[slot].geometry.n_kv_heads; kv_block_at_storage asserts against geometry.n_kv_heads); (2) "each storage slot" must run to storage_slot_count_, so the EAGLE slot at offset logical_slot_count() with layout_t::primary_geometry is constructed too (eagle_storage_offset at 906-908; storage_location accepts offset.i == logical_slot_count via TRON_ASSERT_LE at 730).
    Correction: Omission (partial): the design states the construction requirement and links the code region that holds the hooks ([Allocation source] = kv_cache.hpp#L1187-L1206 covers slot_base and allocate_kv_group; [Block access source] = #L1274-L1334 covers the index formulas), but its prose names neither the per-slot address hook (slot_base, which is arena + n_pages * storage_location(offset).byte_offset) nor the existing per-slot iteration idiom (for_each_active_slot plus the EAGLE branch, as fill_random does at 1057-1065). Suggested addition: "Construction helper: after each successful allocate_kv_group, iterate storage offsets 0 .. storage_slot_count_ (the last one is the EAGLE slot at eagle_storage_offset(), geometry = layout_t::primary_geometry); for each offset whose storage_location names this arena, placement-construct kv_block<page_size, geometry.head_size>[geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages] at slot_base(offset). Reuse the for_each_active_slot + EAGLE pattern from fill_random. In a uniform-geometry layout one array over the whole arena is equivalent. Arenas with 0 bytes need nothing."
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Evidence verified at h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498. (1) allocate_kv_group(bool reclaimable, size_t bytes) at 1199-1208 receives only a byte count and returns early on bytes == 0; it is called for the retained then the reclaimable arena in the book constructor (1101-1102) and for the reclaimable arena alone in restore_reclaimable_kv_storage (968). (2) storage_location(kv_storage_offset, logical_slot_count) at 727-735 returns {reclaimable, byte_offset}; slot_base at 1188-1197 multiplies location.byte_offset by n_pages. (3) kv_block_at_storage at 1311-1334 does reinterpret_cast<kv_block_t*>(base) then blocks[kv_head * n_pages + pg], with kv_block_t = detail::kv_block<page_size, geometry.head_size> per slot geometry; the uniform-reclaimability path at 1284-1289 indexes blocks[(slot.i * n_kv_heads + kv_head) * n_pages + pg] over kv_blocks_alias. (4) make_kv_slot_offsets at 649-670 gives each slot n_kv_heads * block_bytes per page unit, so one slot's region is exactly n_kv_heads * n_pages kv_blocks, contiguous. (5) storage_slot_count_ and support_eagle_ are set in the constructor initializer list (1091-1094) before allocate_kv_group runs, so storage_location and slot_base are usable at the recommended hook point. (6) The design quote is verbatim at design-text.txt line 428; the design names only 'initial allocation', 'restoring a released arena', 'every slot geometry and the extra EAGLE region' and Step 3 'Create the typed objects before access' (line 458); it names no function, loop, or offset. INTENT: Ben #3 says verbatim 'The page arena must construct the actual storage type. We should not obtain typed storage by casting an existing SIMD array to vnni_tensor*.' The finding gives concrete hooks for exactly that, and jhan's prompt says 'Another agent will implement the design by reading this file', so hand-off actionability is in scope. The finding does not push the design away from either intent. It is correctly filed as a note: the design's statement is right, and an implementer reading kv_cache.hpp can find these hooks unaided. I could not see the panel's F12 text, so I cannot confirm C09's paraphrase of F12; that does not affect the finding's value for the implementer.
    Correction: Two refinements to the recommendation, not refutations. (a) The loop must run over physical storage offsets 0..storage_slot_count_-1, not over slot_specs: the EAGLE slot is kv_storage_offset(logical_slot_count()) [kv_cache.hpp:906-910], it has no slot_specs entry, storage_location places it in the retained arena at offsets.values[logical_slot_count].retained, and its block type is kv_block<page_size, layout_t::primary_geometry.head_size> [1326-1329]. The recommendation's 'kv_block<page_size, spec.geometry.head_size>' would skip that slot; the design already says 'the extra EAGLE region'. (b) 'default-construct kv_block<...>[n]' should not be implemented as array placement-new (new (p) T[n]), which may consume unspecified extra bytes; use a per-element placement loop or std::start_lifetime_as_array, which the design already lists as an option.

Note F20. Ben's three straw-man constraints absent from issue scope

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Evidence holds. (1) Ben's comment #3 (conversation comment id 5765866077, comments[3] in issue4525/evidence/github-review.json) contains verbatim: "The page arena must construct the actual storage type. We should not obtain "typed storage" by casting an existing SIMD array to vnni_tensor*."; "The private constructor prevents routine callers from relabelling an arbitrary row-major pointer as packed data."; "There is no data(), pointer conversion, or operator[] that suggests a contiguous row."; "introducing this class must not remove V's zero-initialization invariant or add implicit clearing to hot allocation paths." (2) The live issue #4525 body (gh issue view, updated 2026-09-22T00:11:35Z) has five scope bullets; the two the finding quotes are verbatim. No bullet says the arena must construct the typed storage, that casting the existing SIMD array is forbidden, that the packed view has no public pointer constructor / data() / pointer conversion, or that no clearing may be added to allocation. (3) The design covers all three: design-text.txt line 363 ("Do not expose a public .data member ... Do not allow automatic conversion to a pointer ... Do not expose a public constructor that creates a packed view from a pointer."), line 424 ("Do not cast an existing SIMD-array object to the new owner type."), lines 426-428 and 552 ("Do not use value initialization, which can clear their values." / "Do not clear the arena."). The finding is slightly overstated: the issue bullet "Preserve existing ... initialization rules" is a generic restatement of the zero-initialization invariant, the bullet "Type cache and kernel boundaries so callers cannot confuse packed V with native K" states the goal (not the mechanism) of the private-constructor rule, and the issue links Ben's API sketch directly ("This follows Ben's ... [API sketch]"). The constraint with no trace in the issue text is "arena constructs the storage, no cast of the existing SIMD array", plus the "no added clearing on hot allocation paths" half of the third constraint.
    Correction: Omission in issue #4525 (not in the design): the issue's scope bullets do not state Ben's rule that the page arena must construct the typed storage rather than obtain it by casting the existing SIMD array, nor his rule that no implicit clearing may be added to hot allocation paths. The other constraint (raw pointer must not be relabelled as packed: private constructor, no data(), no pointer conversion) has its goal stated by the bullet "Type cache and kernel boundaries so callers cannot confuse packed V with native K" but not its mechanism, and the zero-initialization invariant is covered generically by "Preserve existing ... initialization rules". The issue also links Ben's API sketch, which holds all three constraints verbatim. The design (sections 5 and 6) covers all three.

Note F31. Column-count rule: relation to Ben's Dims>0 and set_v %32 unstated

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). Every factual leg of F31 re-derives from primary sources at f46e48bab498. (1) set_v rule. h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1623-1641 (uniform-geometry overloads) and 1647-1673 (geometry-templated overloads) all carry requires(... head_size % 32 == 0); the bodies at 2292, 2331, 2372 repeat geometry.head_size % 32 == 0. Confirmed. Side observation that strengthens the finding: all three set_v bodies loop for (i = 0; i < head_size; i += 16) with 16-wide loads (bf16x16 / fp16x16 / fp32x16, lines 2299-2318, 2338-2360, 2380-2398), so the code itself only needs % 16; the % 32 in the requires clause is a stricter contract than the body. When set_v moves into append_v_row, the implementer must choose between the existing contract (% 32) and what the body supports (% 16). The design only says "Keep the existing, stricter shape restrictions for cache stores and weighted sums" (design-text.txt line 190; HTML line 132 has no source link on that paragraph). (2) scaled_v_expr rules. kv_cache.hpp:539 (forward declaration) and 1989 (definition) require head_size % chunk_size == 0; the constructor at 2070-2071 requires head_size == 64 || 128 || 256 || 512. Confirmed. (3) Storage array. kv_cache.hpp:2191 opens struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block; line 2198 (TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 branch) is bf16s v[page_size / 2][head_size / chunk_size * 2]. h/tron/simd/auto.hpp line 35 (CHUNK 16 branch) defines bf16s = bf16x16, so one bf16s is 16 bf16 values and the design's "one SIMD chunk" = 16 values. The storage-level rule head_size % chunk_size == 0 already exists in valid_kv_slot_specs at kv_cache.hpp:587 (if (spec.geometry.head_size % chunk_size != 0) return false;), so the design's type rule matches the existing storage rule and excludes no existing geometry (this also confirms R16's factual claim). (4) Ben's text. evidence/github-review.json, comment 5765866077: struct v_vnni_layout { static_assert(Tokens > 0 && Tokens % 2 == 0); static_assert(Dims > 0); ...} followed by "These restrictions describe storage. Kernels can impose stricter restrictions." Confirmed: Ben required only Dims > 0 for V; the design tightened to chunk-divisible without noting the change (section 2 lists "Simplify" deviations but not this one). (5) Enumeration. 64/128/256/512 satisfy % 16, % 32 and the set membership. 80: 80 % 16 = 0 (passes type rule), 80 % 32 = 16 (fails set_v), not in {64,128,256,512} (fails scaled_v). 48: 48 % 16 = 0, 48 % 32 = 16 (fails set_v). Correct. (6) Design omission. design-text.txt lines 310-336 (append_v_row) state source types and pair semantics but no column rule; lines 256-274 (row.set / matrix.set) state no shape rule; line 412 says only "Keep the supported shapes" for the weighted sum. "Cache stores" is not in the Words-used-here list (lines 21-58); elsewhere the design says "cache append" (line 562) and "Packed V append" (lines 404-406). So an implementer must infer that "cache stores" = set_v and look up the numbers themselves. Citation nits: the finding's range 2191-2198 does contain the v line (2198). R16's cited line 2201 is the TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 branch (bf16s v[page_size][head_size / chunk_size], row-major AVX2 V), not the packed array it quotes; the packed array is line 2198.

The design does say, by reference, which rule the moved set_v bodies carry: "Keep the existing, stricter shape restrictions for cache stores" points at set_v's head_size % 32 == 0 (kv_cache.hpp:1623-1673@f46e48bab498). What is missing is (a) the numeric rules and their source lines, (b) a definition of "cache stores" (the design elsewhere says "cache append"), (c) any shape rule for the new generic row.set / matrix.set (implicitly the type's D % 16 == 0), and (d) a note that Ben's v_vnni_layout required only Dims > 0 and that the chunk-divisible rule equals the existing storage check at kv_cache.hpp:587. All set_v/get_v loop bodies step 16 dimensions, so the % 32 clause is a declared restriction, not a body need. The design covers each piece the finding calls unstated. (1) Type rule: "Its column count must be positive and divisible by the number of values in one SIMD chunk" (design-text.txt line 190) matches the storage array, as the finding itself concedes. (2) append_v_row rule: the same paragraph says "Keep the existing, stricter shape restrictions for cache stores and weighted sums." The design's own terms make "cache stores" the append path: heading "Separate adding cache rows from ordinary assignment" (line 308), "Keep an internal operation named append_v_row to write a newly added cache row" (line 310), step 2 "Move the existing V packing, unpacking, and append conversion code" (line 456), and "Move packed V append logic into the typed operation implementation" (line 440). "Existing" resolves to set_v's % 32 == 0, and the design links that source as S5 "The V write operations convert each supported source type. [V write source]" (line 608). An agent moving the set_v bodies carries their requires clause; no guess is needed. (3) scaled_v rule: "weighted sums" is the design's name for scaled_v ("Check scaled_v, which computes a weighted V sum", line 476), and step 4 says to give scaled_v_expr a typed input while preserving it (line 460), so its {64,128,256,512} constructor constraint stays. (4) row.set / matrix.set: the design scopes the stricter rules to only cache stores and weighted sums, and gives ordinary assignment a different implementation path (generic chunk-wise expression conversion, lines 262-264 and 410: "Existing expression conversion rules. The design's whole subsection at lines 308-336 separates ordinary assignment from cache append, so an agent will not confuse them. (5) The head_size 48/80 case is not a defect: the design's next sentence, "A tensor type can represent a shape that an attention kernel does not support" (line 190), states the layering on purpose, and it matches Ben's own text "These restrictions describe storage. Kernels can impose stricter restrictions." [github-review.json, comment 5765866077]. On the R16 member (Dims > 0 tightened without a flag): Ben called his sketch "a straw-man" and "an API sketch, not a compiled implementation" [same comment]. The design flags the structural deviations Ben raised a reservation about (line 90, "Simplify"). The design justifies the chunk rule indirectly: "replace V's SIMD array with the packed owner. Its size and byte order must match the old array" (line 420) and "Keep sizeof(kv_block), plane sizes, and member offsets" (line 422). R16 concedes the tighter rule is correct and gives no evidence it is wrong, so it attacks a deliberate, justified choice. What remains is polish, not a required change: the design gives the two stricter rules by reference and link rather than as inline numerals (% 32 and {64, 128, 256, 512}). Adding those two numbers to line 190 would help a reader, but the finding's claim that the design "does not say which rule" append_v_row and row.set carry is not supported.

    Correction: The gap is real but smaller than F31 states. Two corrections to the finding text: (1) the design's chunk rule matches an existing, tested book-level rule, not just the array declaration: valid_kv_slot_specs rejects head_size % chunk_size != 0 [kv_cache.hpp:587@f46e48bab498] and t_llama_unit.cpp:431 and :1384 STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE a kv_geometry{1, 65}; so R16's "deviation from Ben's Dims > 0" is the design matching tron's storage rule, and Ben's Dims > 0 would admit geometries the book already rejects. (2) The head_size 48/80 scenario has no real geometry: every head size in C++ model code and tests is 64, 128, 256 or 512 [mock.hpp:91, llama.hpp:147, t_llama_unit.cpp:830-832, heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:60-62@f46e48bab498], and the set_v loop bodies step by 16 [kv_cache.hpp:2299, 2307], so either way an implementer resolves the rule produces correct code for every declared geometry. Recommended change shrinks to: append one sentence with the three file:line pointers after the sentence quoted in design_quote. No table is needed.

Note F43. Typed kernel parameters pull SIMD headers into intrinsics-free header

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: refuted (high confidence, severity note). The cited lines exist as quoted, but the finding misreads what "intrinsics-free" protects, and its recommendation would not compile as written. 1. "No intrinsics" in amx_attn_iface.hpp means AMX intrinsics that need the -mamx-* compile flags, not AVX intrinsics. The header comment ties the statement to "-mamx-tile/-mamx-bf16" [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:2-5@f46e48bab498]. The build file says the same: "The kernel bodies live in one isolated translation unit that alone receives the -mamx-* flags; the rest of the tree sees only the intrinsics-free interface header" [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:184-187@f46e48bab498]. AVX and AVX-512 flags are PUBLIC compile options of the tron target, so every translation unit that can include this header already compiles AVX intrinsics [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:243-246@f46e48bab498]. 2. Every current includer of amx_attn_iface.hpp already sees tron/simd.hpp. self_attention.hpp includes dotter.hpp, expr.hpp and kv_cache.hpp right after the iface header [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:21-27@f46e48bab498]; expr.hpp itself includes tron/simd/bf16.hpp, fp16.hpp, fp32.hpp, int4.hpp [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:8-12@f46e48bab498]. t_amx_numerics.cpp includes dotter.hpp [t/t_amx_numerics.cpp:27@f46e48bab498]. t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp includes model.hpp [t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:41@f46e48bab498]. amx_attn.cpp includes <immintrin.h> [src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp:8@f46e48bab498]. Including the new tensor header from the iface header therefore adds no SIMD exposure to any translation unit, and "any translation unit may include it" stays true. The expr.hpp "precedent" is for circular-dependency avoidance (its own comment, expr.hpp:17), in a header full of SIMD includes, so it is not a precedent for keeping a header SIMD-free. 3. The recommendation "forward-declare ... the view/const_view/seq templates" is not implementable as stated. seq is an alias template (template <size_t... ds> using seq = std::integer_sequence<size_t, ds...>;) [h/tron/tensor/seq.hpp:22-23@f46e48bab498]; alias templates cannot be forward-declared, so seq.hpp must be included (it is SIMD-free: seq.hpp:3-9). The design's 4-argument alias const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>> (design section 5) relies on the default Stride = row_major<Dim> declared at [h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:22-27 and 31-36@f46e48bab498]. A forward declaration without that default, parsed before views.hpp as in self_attention.hpp's include order (iface at line 21, view headers at 22-27), makes the 4-argument template-id ill-formed (too few arguments). Repeating the default in the forward declaration is also ill-formed: a template parameter may not be given a default argument by two declarations in the same scope ([temp.param]/12). 4. What remains true: the design does not state whether amx_attn_iface.hpp includes the tensor header or forward-declares the types (design-text step 6, line 464; step 1 only says "The tensor header must not include an attention kernel header.", line 454; section 8 lists "Unwanted header dependencies" as a failure the three-configuration build detects, line 526). But the natural choice (include the tensor header) compiles within the documented contract and creates no cycle, because step 1 already forbids the reverse direction. An implementing agent would not build the wrong thing. This is a note, not an omission requiring a design change.
    Correction: The design does not say whether amx_attn_iface.hpp will include the new tensor header or forward-declare the view types. Either choice keeps the header's documented contract, because that contract concerns AMX intrinsics and the -mamx-* flags [amx_attn_iface.hpp:2-5, src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:184-187@f46e48bab498], and AVX intrinsics are compiled in every tron translation unit [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:243-246]. If the implementer prefers forward declarations, seq cannot be forward-declared (alias template, seq.hpp:22-23) and the view/const_view forward declarations must not omit or repeat the Stride default (views.hpp:26,35), so including tron/tensor/seq.hpp and tron/tensor/views.hpp (or the new tensor header) is the simpler path.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity minor). The omission is real. I read the whole design text and the design never says how h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp (the AMX kernel interface header) will name vnni_view and const_native_k_view. The only header-hygiene sentences point the other way or are generic: Step 1 "The tensor header must not include an attention kernel header." Step 2 "Add a small internal header only if needed to prevent circular includes." Step 4 "Keep attention policy outside the tensor header." Section 8 lists "Unwanted header dependencies" as a failure the three-configuration build detects. None of these tells the implementing agent whether amx_attn_iface.hpp includes the tensor header or forward-declares the types. The finding's evidence holds at f46e48bab498: amx_attn_iface.hpp:1-5 says "This header contains no intrinsics, so any translation unit may include it"; self_attention.hpp:21 has #include "tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp" // intrinsics-free; view.hpp:7 includes "tron/simd.hpp" (which includes tron/simd/bf16.hpp etc.); expr.hpp:17-22 forward-declares view and const_view. amx_attn_iface.hpp today includes only <cstddef>, <type_traits> and common/numerics/bf16.hpp (lines 109-112). Two corrections to the finding's framing, both from source: (1) The "intrinsics-free" property is about AMX intrinsics, not AVX. src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:186-196 says the kernel TU "alone receives the -mamx-* flags; the rest of the tree sees only the intrinsics-free interface header", and line 245 gives the whole tron target PUBLIC -mavx512f/-mavx512bw/... flags. expr.hpp:27 already uses _mm256_setzero_si256() and every includer of amx_attn_iface.hpp (self_attention.hpp:22-27 via dotter.hpp/kv_cache.hpp, t_amx_numerics.cpp:27 via dotter.hpp, t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:41 via model.hpp) already compiles AVX intrinsics. So including the tensor header would break no build; the cost is header weight plus a header comment that becomes misleading. Severity minor is right, not higher. (2) The recommended forward-declaration recipe is incomplete for const_native_k_view. The design writes it as the 4-argument alias const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>>. That relies on the default Stride = row_major<Dim> declared only in views.hpp:26 and :35 (views.hpp includes expr.hpp, which has the intrinsics). expr.hpp:17-22 forward-declares view/const_view with five parameters and no default, and C++ forbids giving a template parameter a default argument in two declarations in the same scope, so amx_attn_iface.hpp cannot re-declare the default. It must either spell out the stride (row_major<seq<R, C>>, from seq.hpp:394; seq.hpp includes only <algorithm>, <type_traits>, <utility>, common/assert.hpp, common/attributes.hpp, common/numerics/std.hpp, so it is intrinsics-free) next to forward declarations of const_view, or include views.hpp. Only vnni_view, a new type, can be forward-declared without this constraint.
    Correction: Omission stands: Step 6 should say how amx_attn_iface.hpp obtains the parameter type names. Corrected claim: (a) the header's "no intrinsics" rule concerns AMX intrinsics (the -mamx-* flags confined to amx_attn.cpp, src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:186-196); every tron translation unit already compiles AVX-512 intrinsics (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:245, expr.hpp:27), so including the tensor header would not break any build, it would only make the header heavier and its lines 1-5 comment misleading. (b) The forward-declaration route works for the new vnni_view but the design's 4-argument alias const_native_k_view = const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<R,C>> depends on the default Stride = row_major<Dim> declared only in views.hpp:26,35; expr.hpp:17-22 forward-declares view/const_view without that default, and a default template argument cannot be declared twice in one scope, so amx_attn_iface.hpp must either write the stride explicitly (row_major<seq<R,C>> from seq.hpp:394, an intrinsics-free header) beside forward declarations of const_view and seq, or include views.hpp. The design should pick one, and either way should say the header comment at amx_attn_iface.hpp:1-5 stays accurate (no AMX intrinsics) or gets updated.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Every citation in the finding holds. amx_attn_iface.hpp (the interface header for the AMX attention kernels) says at lines 2-5 "This header contains no intrinsics, so any translation unit may include it; the implementations live in src/tron/kernels/amx_attn.cpp, the only translation unit compiled with -mamx-tile/-mamx-bf16" [h/tron/kernels/amx_attn_iface.hpp:2-5@f46e48bab498]. Its include list today is only <cstddef>, <type_traits> and common/numerics/bf16.hpp, and bf16.hpp pulls no intrinsics [amx_attn_iface.hpp:109-112, h/common/numerics/bf16.hpp:3-8, h/common/numerics/std.hpp:3-7@f46e48bab498]. self_attention.hpp includes it with the tag "// intrinsics-free" [h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:21@f46e48bab498]. view.hpp includes tron/simd.hpp [h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:7@f46e48bab498]. expr.hpp forward-declares view and const_view [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:17-22@f46e48bab498]. The design's Step 6 says only "Use typed native-K and packed-V kernel parameters" and names no include strategy, so the omission is real. The finding overstates the consequence. The header comment's stated reason for "no intrinsics" is the -mamx-* compile flags, which only amx_attn.cpp receives [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:184-188@f46e48bab498: "one isolated translation unit that alone receives the -mamx-* flags; the rest of the tree sees only the intrinsics-free interface header"]. AVX2 flags are PUBLIC on the tron target, and AVX-512 flags are PUBLIC under if(AVX512) [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:243-246@f46e48bab498]. Every translation unit that includes amx_attn_iface.hpp links tron: self_attention.hpp is a tron header, amx_attn.cpp is in tron with explicit -mavx512* flags [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:196], and t_amx_numerics and t_amx_dispatch_dtype are registered with LIBRARIES tron [t/CMakeLists.txt:617,620@f46e48bab498]. self_attention.hpp already includes kv_cache.hpp, which includes <immintrin.h> unconditionally [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:12@f46e48bab498]. So including the new tensor header from amx_attn_iface.hpp would compile in every current includer in every build configuration. The implementer cannot build the wrong thing either way. The finding's recommendation is also incomplete as written. The design's alias const_native_k_view<R,C> = const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<R,C>> depends on the default template argument Stride = row_major<Dim>, which is declared in views.hpp [h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:26,35@f46e48bab498] with row_major defined in seq.hpp [h/tron/tensor/seq.hpp:394@f46e48bab498]. The expr.hpp forward declarations carry no defaults, so a forward declaration alone does not make the 4-argument alias valid. seq is an alias template [h/tron/tensor/seq.hpp:21-22@f46e48bab498], so it cannot be forward-declared and seq.hpp must be included. An intrinsics-free route exists (include seq.hpp, forward-declare const_view and vnni_view, spell out sseq<HEAD_SIZE_128,1>), but the finding does not say this. Intent lens: Ben's comments #2/#3 and jhan's prompt say nothing about header hygiene. Ben's straw-man types the kernel signatures and puts the types in h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp. The finding neither advances nor contradicts that intent. The only real consequence is keeping the comment at amx_attn_iface.hpp:2-5 and the "// intrinsics-free" tag at self_attention.hpp:21 truthful, whichever route the implementer takes. That is a code-comment maintenance item, not a design decision.
    Correction: Omission, low consequence: the design's Step 6 does not say whether amx_attn_iface.hpp should include the new tensor header or forward-declare the view types. Either route compiles in every build configuration, because the "intrinsics-free" property protects the -mamx-* flags (only amx_attn.cpp has them), while AVX2/AVX-512 flags are PUBLIC on the tron target and every includer already sees AVX intrinsics through kv_cache.hpp. If the implementer includes the tensor header, the comment at amx_attn_iface.hpp:2-5 and the "// intrinsics-free" tag at self_attention.hpp:21 must be reworded to say "no AMX intrinsics". If the implementer forward-declares, the alias const_native_k_view needs seq.hpp included and the stride spelled out (sseq<HEAD_SIZE_128,1>), because the default Stride = row_major<Dim> lives in views.hpp:26 and a bare forward declaration does not supply it.

Note F54. K is transposed V formula per tile, not per plane

2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Re-derived from primary sources. (1) Design quote is verbatim: design-text.txt line 188 reads "Exchanging V's row and column coordinates alone does not produce this order." (2) k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:101-107 defines index(token, dim) = (((s*4 + c)*16 + dp)*16 + t)*2 + j with s = dim/32, c = token/16, dp = (dim%32)/2, t = token%16, j = dim%2. I enumerated all 64 x 128 = 8192 positions against the design's packed-V formula (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dim + token%2 with token and dimension exchanged over the whole plane (rows = 128 dims, cols = 64 tokens): 7680 mismatches, 512 matches. That reproduces the finding's number, so the design sentence is correct because of "alone". (3) Tile-wise: with tile base (s*4 + c)*512 and the transposed packed-V formula applied inside the tile (rows = 32 dims, cols = 16 tokens), all 8192 positions match. The same result follows from the byte-address code, not only from index(): pair_base (k_vnni.hpp:113-123) places token t at dword (s*4 + c)*256 + t and pair_row_offsets (126-132) steps the 16 pair rows by 16 dwords, so the local element index is dp*32 + 2*t + j, identical to (d/2)*(2*16) + 2*t + d%2 for d = 2*dp + j. (4) The dword-sharing statement holds: k_vnni.hpp:46-47 "The two values of one dimension pair share one 32-bit lane"; j = dim%2 is the innermost term. (5) The design does not state the per-tile relation anywhere (grep of design-text.txt for panel/tile/16 tokens/32 dimensions hits only lines 31, 188, 488); line 488 says "Keep the blocked K mapping inside this view. Keep it separate from packed V's mapping.", so the recommended sentence adds a fact and contradicts no design decision. (6) Recommended wording "tiles laid out dimension-step major, token-block minor" matches the tile base (s*4 + c)*512 with s = dimension step and c = token block (k_vnni.hpp:22-27 header comment). One qualification, which does not refute: the sharable helpers are limited to bf16-pair-in-a-dword packing (even value in the low half, odd in the high half). Row-level operations differ, because a K logical row (one token) is one dword per line across 64 lines and is written without read-modify-write (k_vnni.hpp:28-30, scatter_row 139-146), while a V logical row is interleaved inside a token pair and odd-token writes read-modify-write the pair (kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498 set_v, per facts.md). Severity "note" is right: informational, no design change required.
    Correction: The factual claims stand as written. Sharpen the benefit clause so an implementer does not over-read it: the relation lets vnni_view and k_vnni_view share the bf16-pair-in-a-dword pack/unpack helpers (low half = even element, high half = odd element), but not the row-level operations, because a K token row is 64 dwords spread over 64 lines and is written without read-modify-write (k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-30, 139-146), while a V token row is interleaved within a token pair and odd-token writes read-modify-write the pair.
  • design-text lens: refuted (high confidence, severity note). The finding's arithmetic holds, but the design already covers the point and the finding's only rationale for adding a sentence contradicts a deliberate design choice without evidence. Arithmetic check (my own script, k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:101-107 index formula vs the design's packed-V formula): a whole-plane transpose of packed V mismatches K at 7680 of 8192 positions; inside each 16-token x 32-dimension tile (base (s*4+c)*512) K equals the packed-V formula applied to the transposed tile (32 dim rows x 16 token columns) at 8192 of 8192 positions; tile bases are 512 for (s=0,c=1) and 2048 for (s=1,c=0), so tiles are dimension-step major, token-block minor. The finding's claim and its proposed sentence are correct as mathematics. Design-text fairness: (1) Section 4 line 186 gives the complete blocked-K formula, from which the tile fact follows directly; the implementer needs nothing more to compute addresses. (2) The criticized sentence (line 188, "Exchanging V's row and column coordinates alone does not produce this order.") is conceded correct by the finding itself. (3) Section 5 line 302 explicitly excludes "General transposes (operations that exchange rows and columns)" from the initial implementation. (4) Section 7 line 488 is a deliberate decision: "Keep the blocked K mapping inside this view. Keep it separate from packed V's mapping." The finding's sole stated benefit, "helps the implementer see which pair helpers can be shared between vnni_view and k_vnni_view", pushes against line 488 and offers no evidence that sharing is better. Its premise is also weak: V's pair helpers (kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498:2289-2320, set_v) zero-extend the even token's values so the odd partner becomes 0, or load the pair, ternarylogic-merge the odd token's values, and store; they exist because two tokens share one dword and writes are read-modify-write across tokens. K's dword holds two dimensions of the same token, loaded directly from the source row and scattered with no partner handling (k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:139-147 scatter_row). The header comment at k_vnni.hpp:28-30 states this explicitly: "tokens are written independently (no read-modify-write between tokens, unlike the pair-interleaved V plane)." So none of V's row-write pair helpers has a K counterpart; the only coincidence is one line of in-tile index arithmetic, which the design chose to keep separate. No design change is needed.
    Correction: The tile-level transposition is arithmetically true (verified 8192/8192 with tiles laid out dimension-step major, token-block minor), but it does not identify shareable pair helpers: V's pair helpers merge or zero the other token's half of a dword (kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498:2289-2320), while K's dword holds two dimensions of one token loaded directly from the row (k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-30, 139-147). Only the one-line in-tile index arithmetic coincides, and the design deliberately keeps the K and V mappings separate (design line 488). Optional at most; no change required.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). The geometric fact in F54 holds; I re-derived it. With R = 64 tokens and D = 128 dimensions, swapping token and dimension in the packed-V formula over the whole plane disagrees with the blocked-K formula at 7680 of 8192 positions. Applying the packed-V formula inside each (dimension step s, token block c) tile, on a 32-row x 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, at element base (s*4 + c)*512, matches all 8192 positions. The tile index s*4 + c is dimension-step major, token-block minor. So the design sentence "Exchanging V's row and column coordinates alone does not produce this order." [design-text.txt:188] is correct, as the finding says. The child code describes the same relation in its own words: "One 64-byte row holds that dimension pair for 16 consecutive tokens" and the static_assert text "the block save transposes 16 token rows onto the 16 pair rows of a panel" [h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:16-17, 83-84; formula at :22-26 and :101-107]. Two parts of the finding do not hold and are corrected below. (1) The stated benefit, "helps the implementer see which pair helpers can be shared between vnni_view and k_vnni_view", is unsupported. In K the dword pair is two dimensions of the same token, written in one row store: "tokens are written independently (no read-modify-write between tokens, unlike the pair-interleaved V plane)" [k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:28-30]. The design's V pair helpers set_pair and append_v_row exist to preserve or zero the other token of a pair written at another time [design-text.txt:323-332], so they have no K counterpart. The design also directs, for the child PR, "Keep the blocked K mapping inside this view. Keep it separate from packed V's mapping." [design-text.txt:488] and excludes general transposes from the initial implementation [design-text.txt:302]. Blocked K is "PR #4424 only" [design-text.txt:186]. (2) The recommended sentence leaves the shape of the transposed V formula unstated; the finding's evidence labels it "R=16, D=32" (the K tile shape), and an implementer applying the V formula with 32 columns would get positions above 512 and a mismatch. The corrected sentence names the shape. Intent check: Ben's straw-man keeps two separate layout structs, k_vnni_layout and v_vnni_layout, each with its own offset(); jhan's prompt asks that the layout be the view. The corrected note is descriptive only and does not move the design away from either. It has no consequence for the parent PR (blocked K is out of its scope) or for Ben's acceptance, so severity stays at note.
    Correction: Keep as a note with this wording: "Within each tile of 16 tokens x 32 dimensions (dimension step s, token block c), at element base (s*4 + c)*512, the blocked K offset equals the packed-V formula applied to a 32-row x 16-column plane with row = dimension % 32 and column = token % 16, that is (dimension % 32 / 2)*32 + 2*(token % 16) + dimension % 2. Tiles are ordered dimension-step major, token-block minor." Drop the claim that this lets pair helpers be shared between the V view and the K view: K writes a whole token row at once and needs no partner-preserving or partner-zeroing write, and the design keeps the K mapping separate from the V mapping [design-text.txt:488]. State that the sentence applies to the child PR only.

Note F56. kv_block size and alignment constraints hold with alignas(64) owner

3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it

  • source lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Every cited line holds at main f46e48bab498. h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1143 reads static constexpr size_t storage_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment);. kv_cache.hpp:2186 reads static const size_t kv_block_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment);. kv_cache.hpp:2191-2205 declare struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block with alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size], the CHUNK-16 alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s v[page_size / 2][head_size / chunk_size * 2], and static_assert(sizeof(k) == sizeof(v));. kv_cache.hpp:1315-1319 (kv_block_at_storage) read constexpr size_t expected_block_bytes = detail::KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 * page_size * geometry.head_size * sizeof(bf16); static_assert(sizeof(kv_block_t) == expected_block_bytes); static_assert(alignof(kv_block_t) == storage_alignment);, and kv_cache.hpp:648 sets KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 = 2. h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:86-87 read chunk_size = TRON_CHUNK_SIZE; chunk_alignment = chunk_size * sizeof(float);, with TRON_CHUNK_SIZE = 16 under TRON_AVX512_ENABLED and 8 otherwise (auto.hpp:17-21). So chunk_alignment is 64 bytes (16 lanes) or 32 bytes (8 lanes), and both alignment constants are 64 bytes in both builds, as the finding says. Size arithmetic also holds: bf16s is __m256i (32 bytes) under 16 lanes and __m128i (16 bytes) under 8 lanes (h/tron/simd/types.hpp:25-26), so sizeof(k) = page_size * head_size * 2 bytes, and an owner holding one alignas(64) std::array of page_size * head_size bf16 values matches it exactly. page_size is fixed at 64 (kv_cache.hpp:1455), so the owner size is 128 * head_size bytes, a multiple of 64 bytes for any head_size; the finding's qualifier "head_size >= 64" is a sufficient but unnecessary condition, not an error. Any non-empty extra member (pointer, stored view) would round the owner up by at least 64 bytes and break both the expected_block_bytes assert and sizeof(k) == sizeof(v). The design quote is verbatim at design-text.txt line 422 ("Keep sizeof(kv_block), plane sizes, and member offsets required by the existing allocation calculations. Keep 64-byte alignment."). No contrary evidence found; the finding is a correct note.
    Correction: Minor precision only: because page_size is fixed at 64 tokens (kv_cache.hpp:1455@f46e48bab498), the owner's size is 128 * head_size bytes and is a multiple of 64 bytes for every head_size, so the qualifier "for every supported head_size >= 64" can be dropped.
  • design-text lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). I checked each cited line at main f46e48bab498 with git show. All hold. (1) h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1143 reads static constexpr size_t storage_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment);, and line 1203 passes std::align_val_t(storage_alignment) to the arena allocation, so it is the arena alignment. (2) kv_cache.hpp:2186 reads static const size_t kv_block_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment);. (3) h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:86-87 read chunk_size = TRON_CHUNK_SIZE; chunk_alignment = chunk_size * sizeof(float);, and auto.hpp:18/20 define TRON_CHUNK_SIZE as 16 or 8 only, so chunk_alignment is 64 or 32 bytes and both max() values are 64 bytes. (4) kv_cache.hpp:2191-2205 declare struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block with alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size], under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s v[page_size / 2][head_size / chunk_size * 2], and static_assert(sizeof(k) == sizeof(v));. (5) kv_cache.hpp:1315-1319 (the finding says 1314-1319) compute expected_block_bytes = detail::KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 * page_size * geometry.head_size * sizeof(bf16) with KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 = 2 (line 648), then static_assert(sizeof(kv_block_t) == expected_block_bytes); static_assert(alignof(kv_block_t) == storage_alignment);. (6) page_size is fixed at 64 (kv_cache.hpp:1455 inline static const size_t page_size = 64;), so the V plane is 64 * head_size * 2 bytes = 128 * head_size bytes. An owner with one alignas(64) std::array of that many bf16 values and no other member therefore has sizeof equal to the plane size (no tail padding) and alignof 64, and both static_asserts plus sizeof(k) == sizeof(v) keep holding. Adding any extra member (pointer, view) rounds the size up by 64 bytes and breaks the asserts at lines 1318 and 2205. Design-text fairness: the finding is confirmatory ("no change required"), and the design already states the constraint ("Keep sizeof(kv_block), plane sizes, and member offsets required by the existing allocation calculations. Keep 64-byte alignment."; "Its size and byte order must match the old array."; "Add no allocation or pointer for either plane."; "Do not keep a view that points to the owner's own array."), so the finding and the design agree. Nothing in the finding is wrong or unsupported.
    Correction: Two small precisions, no change of verdict. (a) The qualifier "for every supported head_size >= 64" is unnecessary: page_size is fixed at 64 (kv_cache.hpp:1455), so the plane size 128 * head_size bytes is a multiple of 64 bytes for every integer head_size. (b) The optional rule "the owner holds one array and no other member" is already partly stated by the design ("Add no allocation or pointer for either plane" and "Do not keep a view that points to the owner's own array"); only the explicit link to the sizeof static_assert at kv_cache.hpp:1318 is new.
  • intent lens: holds (high confidence, severity note). Every cited line holds at f46e48bab498. h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1143 static constexpr size_t storage_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment); and :2186 static const size_t kv_block_alignment = std::max<size_t>(64, chunk_alignment);. h/tron/simd/auto.hpp:86-87 chunk_size = TRON_CHUNK_SIZE; chunk_alignment = chunk_size * sizeof(float); with TRON_CHUNK_SIZE 16 or 8 (auto.hpp:18,20), so chunk_alignment is 64 or 32 bytes and both maxima are 64 bytes in both builds. kv_cache.hpp:2191-2205 shows struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block with alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size], the CHUNK-16 bf16s v[page_size / 2][head_size / chunk_size * 2], and static_assert(sizeof(k) == sizeof(v));. kv_cache.hpp:1316-1319 shows expected_block_bytes = detail::KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 * page_size * geometry.head_size * sizeof(bf16) plus the two static_asserts on sizeof and alignof. Two premises the finding uses without citing also hold: KV_BLOCK_PLANES_2 is 2 (kv_cache.hpp:648), page_size is 64 (kv_cache.hpp:1455), and storage_alignment is indeed the arena allocation alignment (kv_cache.hpp:1203 std::align_val_t(storage_alignment) in allocate_kv_group). An extra 8-byte pointer member would round sizeof up by 64 bytes and break the sizeof assert, as the finding says. Intent check: Ben's straw-man in comment #3 (evidence/github-review.json) declares class alignas(64) vnni_tensor whose only member is std::array<bf16, Layout::elements> data_;, so the finding's 'one array, no other member' rule matches Ben's proposal, and the design already says 'vnni_tensor contains one array of bf16 values inside the tensor object' and 'Do not keep a view that points to the owner's own array' (design-text.txt lines 238, 270). The finding confirms the design, recommends no change, and pushes nothing away from Ben's or jhan's intent. One nit that does not change the verdict: the finding attributes the multiple-of-64 property to head_size >= 64, while the direct reason is page_size = 64 (128 bytes per dimension column); the stated condition is still true for every supported head_size.
